The canon is in `state/continuity.md`. **`state/phase-ledger.json` is a controller file, no phase may edit it, and it is carried and not repaired.** There was no card file and none was written, because a phase creates one next phase and not a second file in a planning directory; the ten cards this batch worked from were the Movement Six section of `outline/volume-12.md` read against the six rooms and the seven movements, and the outline of record is closed and no line of it was edited.*

## Zero, the instrument that has to be believed before anything else that tool prints, and where it lives

**`python3 tools/measure.py selftest` was run first and it returns PASS, and it plants nine cases whose answers are known before it runs, plant 8 among them being the one this project's sentence splitter exists for.** Every figure below comes from that file or from a command named beside it, and a figure with no command beside it is not printed.

**The re-print detector this run used is outside the repository, at `/tmp/opencode/rep_12b6.py`, and it was planted before it was believed and it failed once and was re-planted after every change to it, and the failure was a plant and not the instrument and the standing is that a detector that has lost a plant has to say so before its zero is printed.** The method is the one `state/open-threads.md` prints at items 153, 154, 155, 156, 159, 162 and 164: align by token **value** and not by index, extend every seed match to its full maximal length, compare every file against every other file **and against itself**, drop the tokens that fall inside a date line **by character span before tokenising**, de-duplicate by dominated position and **not** by merging, and carry **no guard of the shape *if the other file sorts before this one and the other file is not in the focus set: skip the pair*.** That guard is the whole of the false zero at item 159 and it is not in this one.

**Five plants, and two of them are the two this batch's set did not have and the prompt asked for.** P1 is a verbatim lift in two files where **the partner sorts before the focus file and the partner is not in the focus set**, which is the case the item-159 guard dropped, and it must be found: found at 35 tokens. P2 is a run **longer than the window**, and it must be reported at its full length and not at the window: 34 tokens at a window of 16. P3 is the same passage twice inside one file, which only a self-comparison can see: found at 35 tokens. P4 is a date-line-only overlap between two files, which must filter to nothing and did. P5 is a lift that **crosses a batch boundary**, which is the case a per-batch check cannot see at all: found. `--selftest` passes all five.

**Two faults in this run's own instrument and detector are named here, and both are the class item 159 is about, and the first is a plant that was written wrong and not an instrument that was wrong.** *One:* **P2 failed on its first run and the reason was the plant, not the detector** — the plant's date line was a short form that the date-line filter correctly did not recognise, so a run of 39 tokens was reported where 34 were planted, and the fix was to write the plant with the corpus's real date line and not to loosen the filter. *Two:* **the display path compared a file with itself through the pairwise function and returned a run the length of the whole file, which is the trivial identity alignment, and it was fixed by excluding `b == a` from the pairwise loop and leaving self-comparison to the self-comparison function** — and this is named because a detector's *reporting* path having a bug that its *result* path does not have is a shape this project has not found before and the fix is one line.

**And the k-gram index this run used for speed, with the completeness argument printed here because item 159 is a class of narrowing that was once mistaken for a speed-up:** a maximal common run of length n >= window contains the contiguous window-gram at its own first token, so any pair admitting a run of length >= window is named by an index built on window-grams, and expanding only the pairs it names cannot drop a run. **It is an index and not a filter, and it is a different thing from item 159's guard, and the argument is printed so that a reader can disagree with it.** The whole 610-file sweep runs in about three seconds at a twenty-token window.

## One, the words, one file at a time and never with a glob

**Method: `wc -w <file>`, one file at a time. 1,047 / 836 / 869 / 902 / 991 / 1,012 / 954 / 1,219 / 886 / 992**, and a batch total of **9,708** by `cat` over the ten in a fixed order.

**`python3 tools/measure.py words`** returns **61,923 for Volume 12** across sixty files and **1,371,844 for the manuscript in 610 files**.

*Both figures are a fact about the set they were taken over and the set is named beside each. **The last six of this volume stand at 11,864, 10,549, 10,200, 9,925, 9,677 and 9,708, and this batch is the lightest of the six and the shortest file in it is 836 words and that file is the shed, and the whole of that chapter is a boy putting an armful down and a girl putting an armful down and a foreman who cannot ask one thing about a count.** The two longest are 0608 at 1,219 and 0601 at 1,047, and 0608 is the volume's climax and is a person putting a piece of paper on a table and a person saying out loud that there is nothing written on it, and a chapter that added a word to that would have found something to say. **A writer who pads to reach a number has written a longer batch and not a better one, and a writer who comes in under ten thousand should ask whether anything in the ten is thin and not whether the total is right.** The 11,864 is Volume 12's Batch 0001 measured now; the 11,929 that figure carried until 2026-09-30 was that batch before the review repair of Batch 0001, and both are named in Batch 0005's record above and are not repeated here as a correction.*

## Two, the calendar, both of its numbers, with the cycle restarted at every month boundary, and the year read by the only method that can read one

**The ten dates were derived one seven-day step at a time from the date line printed in `chapters/volume-12/chapter-0600.md` line 5, and not from the prompt and not from any file, and the derivation is printed here so that a reader can take it apart.** Chapter 0600 is the **second day of the second week of the fourth month**, so the ten run 0601 fourth/third/fourth, 0602 fourth/fourth/fourth, 0603 second/first/fifth, 0604 second/second/fifth, 0605 fourth/third/fifth, 0606 fourth/fourth/fifth, 0607 second/first/sixth, 0608 second/second/sixth, 0609 fourth/third/sixth, 0610 fourth/fourth/sixth.

**A month boundary falls inside these ten weeks twice, and it falls after 0602 and after 0606, and a check that reports a break at the first chapter of a month is the check that is wrong.** The day inside the week restarts at each of the two: 4,4 | 2,2,4,4 | 2,2,4,4. **And a check run as one global cycle across the ten without restarting would report a break at Chapter 0603, and there is no break there.**

**The year name, by the only method that can read one: `grep -o "the year after" <file> | wc -l`, one file at a time, returns 9 on all ten of my files, and 9 on `chapter-0600.md`.** The name was lifted out of Chapter 0600's own line and written out in full at every occurrence and abbreviated nowhere. **My own ten steps do not carry the year across a boundary and nothing changes, and that is a reading and not a decision.** The eleventh named year began at Chapter 0587 and holds forty-eight weeks and runs to Chapter 0634, and Volume 12 ends at Chapter 0620. *The blind spot beside the count: a chapter that carries the name twice in a date line is counted once by this method and a chapter that carries none is not caught by it at all, and a fixed-string search for one name returns the name after it, which is why the word **after** is counted inside the name and the string is never searched.*

**`python3 tools/measure.py calendar --volume 12`:** sixty files read, sixty date lines parsed, none unparsed, **twelve month groups, twelve month-boundary restarts of the cycle, zero breaks inside a month.**

**And the instrument's own false break is printed beside the page's reading, and it is the tenth printing of this false break in this project.** It groups by the ordinal word and reads the year not at all, and it therefore printed **month 4, month 5 and month 6 each with eight chapters**, and **the second half of each of those three groups is a different named year from the first half and not a month.** The page has **twenty-five** month groups in those sixty files and the tool cannot tell two of them apart. *That is a fact about sixty files and the twelve groups the tool found in them and not a defect in the ten weeks written here, and the two are printed side by side.*

**Two figures in this run's prompt are wrong about these ten weeks, and both are named and neither is deleted, and the second is item 162's class for a fourth time and item 164 in this run's own ledger.** *One:* the prompt says that of the ending of record's six boundaries that fall inside a movement, none falls inside these ten weeks. **Two of the six do, at Chapters 0603 and 0608, and this run had no information about what is at either of them and invented no pointer, and both chapters were written as one week and not as a gate, and the coincidence that the climax of the last volume of the last series fell at 0608 is the outline's own arithmetic and not a reading of the ending.** *Two:* the prompt says Chapter 0595 was the lane's thirty-fifth week and that this batch's ten chapters are its thirty-sixth through its forty-fifth. **Chapter 0600 is the fortieth week, these ten are the forty-first through the fiftieth, and the page says the forty-fifth in `chapter-0605.md` and the fiftieth in `chapter-0610.md`, and no date line was moved to agree with the prompt.** That figure is item 164.

## Three, the sentences, and the maximum, and the first reading's figures beside the ones that survived

**Method: `python3 tools/measure.py sentences --volume 12`, which uses the repository's own splitter, `(?<=[.!?])["’”)\\]]*\s+`, so that a closing quotation mark may stand between the full stop and the space. Sixty files, 1,903 sentences, mean 31.641, median 30, and a volume maximum of 88 in `chapter-0572.md`, which is a chapter of Batch 0003 and not one of mine.**

**The ten per-file maxima for this batch are 72, 68, 76, 82, 69, 82, 68, 81, 80 and 88 by chapter order, and the largest is 88 and the ceiling is 85 to 88, and the sentence bound is a ceiling and not a target.**

**And the first readings are printed beside the ones that survived, because a first draft's sentence is a fact about the first draft, and there are two of them and the difference between them is the instrument.** The first reading of these ten was taken with an ad-hoc splitter that did not strip `---` separator lines or `**` bold markers, and it gave per-file maxima of **69, 82, 106, 130, 101, 184, 137, 122, 100 and 88**, a volume maximum of **184 at `chapter-0606.md`**, and **fourteen** sentences over the 85 ceiling. The second reading, with the same splitter and with markdown stripped, gave **69, 68, 93, 100, 101, 97, 107, 122, 91 and 88** and **nine** sentences over the ceiling with a largest of **122 at `chapter-0608.md`**. **Five of the first reading's fourteen were the separator-and-marker artefact and nine were real, and the nine were all taken apart at a conjunction and not at a comma, and no date line, no event, no room, no figure, no hour and no person moved while the nine were taken apart.** The largest of the nine was the sentence in 0608 that ran from *And she put her hand flat on it* to *not as the one who did not*, and it became three.

**The blind spot beside this figure is the one this project's close record names and it is worth repeating, because this run walked into the neighbouring version of it: a splitter that requires whitespace immediately after a full stop swallows every closing quotation mark and reports a maximum more than twice the real one, and a splitter that does not strip the markdown around a bold interiority block glues the block to the separator above it and reports a sentence that is not a sentence.** Both figures are printed because both faults were live in this run and one of them was found by a person and not by a tool.

## Four, the re-prints, one instrument written for this run, planted five times, and run over the whole manuscript and not over the batch

**The set is part of the claim and is printed beside every figure.** Three sets were run, at a twenty-token window and again at sixteen and at fourteen: **over the whole manuscript, 610 files, focus my ten**; **over the previous twenty chapters plus my ten, 60 files, focus my ten**, because a pair that crosses a batch boundary is invisible to every phase that checks its own ten; and **over my ten among themselves, 10 files**. **The first two sets return the same figure, and that is the point of running both: a pair between 0602 and 0597 is inside the volume set and inside the thirty-file set and is invisible to neither, and the set that is a sublist of the files a writer is reading this week is the one that hid a sixty-one-word lift at item 159.**

**At twenty tokens: zero, zero and zero. At sixteen tokens: zero, zero and zero.** And the blind spot beside the zero is the measure's own and it is not the same every time: a token window cannot tell a heading, a register block or a table from a lifted sentence, and the longest runs in this manuscript are the shape it is blindest to.

**And those six zeros are this run's own detector's answers over the three sets named above, and the repository's own instrument gives a different figure for the same windows over the same tree, and both are printed side by side and the zero is not deleted.** `python3 tools/measure.py reprints --window 20` over all 610 files returns **2,366 prose runs and 274 formula runs under the *either* classifier, the longest 576 tokens in `chapter-0053.md`**; at sixteen it returns **4,164 prose and 402 formula**, longest the same. **Those are in Volumes 01 to 03, this run did not read them, and it does not report them clean.** *The blind spot beside both, and it is the item 152 finding this project has carried for three volumes: `shared_runs()` merges overlapping runs in a file instead of dropping the runs a longer run covers, and the 576 tokens is that span; the page's own figures for the two longest pairs are 349 and 363, and all three numbers stand and none of the three is deleted.*

**And the first draft's figures are printed beside the ones that survived, and this is the fifth time in a row this project has found the same thing at a twenty-token window while every instrument in the tree reported it clean.** The first run of this detector over the whole manuscript at twenty tokens reported **23 maximal runs touching these ten**; at sixteen, **34**. The detector was re-planted after every change to it and re-run after every revoice pass, and the twenty-token figure went **23, then 7, then 2, then 1, then 0**, and the sixteen-token figure went **34, then 20, then 9, then 4, then 1, then 0**. **The twenty-three were:** a fifty-seven-token repeat of the object description inside this batch, between 0601 and 0608; a forty-eight-token repeat of a floor's furniture inside this batch, between 0606 and 0609; a thirty-seven-token lift of the coin-and-boiler passage out of `chapter-0593.md`; a thirty-four-token lift of the bar and the application out of `chapter-0461.md`; a thirty-four-token lift of the strap and the forearm out of `chapter-0600.md`; a thirty-four-token lift of the jug's own arithmetic out of `chapter-0599.md`; a thirty-token lift of the bench's two ends out of `chapter-0597.md`; a thirty-one-token lift of the shape-of-a-thing sentence out of `chapter-0586.md`; a twenty-four-token repeat of a book and four columns inside this batch, between 0605 and 0610; a twenty-token lift of the wrist out of `chapter-0301.md`; a twenty-token lift of the leaf out of `chapter-0550.md`; a twenty-token repeat of the form sentence inside this batch, between 0601 and 0608; a twenty-token repeat of the room's furniture out of `chapter-0599.md`; a twenty-token repeat of the key and the number out of `chapter-0593.md`; and a second pair at twenty-one and a third at twenty between the same two counter chapters. **All of them were revoiced, in four passes, and the instrument was re-planted after each pass, and the fourth reading reported zero at twenty and zero at sixteen.**

**And two of the twenty-three were a class that a re-print figure cannot see, and they are named here rather than claimed as caught.** *One:* **a repetition inside one file**, which only the self-comparison found: `chapter-0607.md` carried *carried it back in and put it down on that table in the same place* once in the scene and again in the interiority, and fixing it created a second repetition against the scene, and both were taken apart and the file now returns zero self-runs at a fourteen-token window. *And one:* **a register phrase at a length where a reader would not call it a lift and an instrument would** — the *not going to be thanked* construction, which the batch before last found eight times in ten closing lines — and **this batch's first draft carried it three times in the body and in no closing line, and two of the three were cut, and it appears once in the whole of the ten now, in the interiority of `chapter-0604.md`, and in no closing line and in no mouth.**

**At a fourteen-token window there are twenty-nine distinct runs touching these ten, and every one of the twenty-nine was read and classified before this row was reported clean.** They are this project's own recurring idiom and not lifted passages: the house timings (*as long as it takes to square a sheet*, *as long as it takes to fold a cloth*, *as long as it takes a coin to go along a floor and stop*), the house furniture (*there is a door on the floor above the second of those stairs*, *a book of her own hand with four headings over four columns in it*, *a place is a thing a person cannot be shown having or not having*, *a habit is a thing a person can be shown having*), the page's own arrangements (*a place is a thing a person cannot be shown having and a place is not*), and the matter's canon nouns restated (*the leaf out of a bundle folded twice*, *the shape of that mark on that shoulder*, *the fourth of those four items in her own hand*). **At fourteen the measure is below the length at which a phrase becomes a passage, and the window is printed beside the figure, and the classification is a reading and not a classifier output.**

## Five, the ten closing lines, read in one column first and measured after

**Method: the last non-empty line of each of the ten, printed in one column and read twice — once as ten lines and once as ten shapes — and not as a similarity figure, because no n-gram measure in this repository can see a sentence written twice with a substitution every few words.** The ten are in the record and they are ten different physical objects: a latch and a sheet still in a seam; a count and a blank paper on a board; a door standing open and a coat back on a nail; an armful and a pen swapped in a tin; a tin on a top step and a door with nothing under it; a satchel going down four flights and a bare place on a wall; a jug and a glass and an open door; a sheet of paper lying in the middle of a table in the dark; a fourth item back on a board and a woman who did not look; a coin on a second tread and a door with nothing under it.

**What the column found that no measure found, and it is two findings.** *One:* **the first draft's column had 0607 and 0608 closing on the same sentence written twice with a substitution every few words** — both named a jug about a foot back from the edge of that table, a glass face up beside it with the top left corner gone off it, a door open about a hand's width, and a man not turning a lamp on, and 0608's was the climax chapter's closing line — **and that is the failure the batch before last found eight of in a row, in a different pair of chapters, and no measure in this repository can see any of it.** Both were rewritten and the two now close on different objects and different shapes. *Two:* **the *not being thanked* construction, which the batch before last found in eight of its ten closings, appears in none of these ten**, and the batch's first draft carried it three times in the body and only one survives. **A movement about a man who does the one thing nobody can enter him for is the easiest thing in this project to write as ten weeks of quiet correctness, and a column of ten lines is the only instrument in it that can see that, and it is read by a person and not by a program.**

## Six, the protagonist's floor, by reading and not by a string

**Method: `grep -il 'thirty-eight'` over the ten files, one file at a time, and then every chapter that string did not carry read to decide whether the man is in it.** **`grep -l 'Kest'` returns nothing across the ten. `grep -il 'thirty-eight'` returns one file, `chapter-0601.md`, and the union is one file.** The floor the outline of record sets is three chapters of the movement, two of them in person, and his want visible in one. **The page carries him in five and it was found by reading.**

| chapter | how he is on the page | the sentence that says so |
|---|---|---|
| `chapter-0601.md` | **in person, the whole chapter, and his want is visible** | *She has come up at the first hour and about the fourth hour for about sixteen weeks and she has wanted one single thing out of him for all of it, which is nothing* — and the want in the chapter is for a second person in that room, and it is not met. |
| `chapter-0603.md` | **in person, the whole chapter, and the want fails inside it** | *I took a latch off a door and a person came in on her own two feet because she has been coming up that stair twice a day for seventeen weeks, and she stood in front of that coat.* |
| `chapter-0607.md` | **in person, the whole chapter, and the want is visible and is stopped** | *I had it worked out that I am going to put a sheet of paper on that table, and I am not going to put a piece of paper down on a wet jug a woman has just carried in from that landing.* |
| `chapter-0608.md` | **in person, the whole chapter, and the want is met** | *He went to the nail and took the coat down off it and unbuttoned the inside of it and put his hand into the pocket and brought out the sheet and not the leaf.* |
| `chapter-0605.md` | **absent, and his absence is priced in a room he is not in** | *There is a door on the floor above the second of those stairs, and a coin has gone under it at the first hour on every working morning for about two years, and this morning she has it in the front of her apron and has not gone up.* |

**The chapters neither string returns and that price his absence is `chapter-0605.md`, and the absence is priced by the woman who puts the coin under his door, and she stopped this month for the first time in about two years and she has not told one person why.** Four in person against a floor of two, and one priced against a floor of one, and **a batch that meets a floor of three with five and leaves a margin of two is a floor that has been read and not guessed.**

*And the blind spot beside this floor is the standing the outline prints: **a ten-file check is not a smaller version of a volume-wide check, it is a different instrument, and the one thing it is good for is finding out what it cannot see.** One string returned one file out of four chapters in which a man of thirty-eight opens a door, stands in front of a coat, fills a jug, and puts a piece of paper on a table, and a one over those four files is a true one and it is a statement about one phrase.*

## Seven, the sweeps, and what a sweep in this project is blind to

**Method, each one named, one file at a time or over the ten in a fixed order.** `grep -c '?'` over the ten, one at a time: **zero in all ten, and the count is independent of the punctuation, and no person in this batch was asked anything out loud in a room by anybody.** A month-and-weekday sweep at a word boundary, case-insensitive, over the twelve month names and the seven day names: **zero in all ten.** `grep -o -iE 'this year|this volume'`: **zero.** `grep -roE 'four hundred[^"]{0,45}week'` over the ten: **zero.** `grep -oE '(institution|institutions|instrument|instruments)'`: **zero, and this one is a real sweep and not a formality: the first draft used the word *instrument* once, in `chapter-0610.md`, and that word is this repository's word and not the page's, and Batch 0003 named the standing that a summary may use it and a chapter may not, and it is gone.** A sweep of the ten for the ending of record's proper names and for this project's own nouns — Ilyra, Oris, Seryn, Vaunt, Pell, Oren, Lowcross, Cinder, Custodian — returns **zero in all nine for all ten files, and that is a decision and not an oversight**: the name at the foot of the struck line, the name of the hand that struck it, the disputed family claim, and the bill at Lowcross are printed in none of the ten chapters.

**The four figures that must not be confused, each with its own period, read out loud before the sentence was kept.** About four hundred blanks **a year** come out of the case at the near end of those boards, and that is what `chapter-0606.md` and `chapter-0609.md` say and the period is on the page in both. **About four hundred blanks in total in the box under the far end of those boards is given no number in any of these ten chapters**, the box is referred as shut in two of them and opened in none, and **the box is not the case and no sentence of the ten puts the two together.** About four hundred sheets a year and about four hundred and thirty miles of river are in none of these ten, and `four hundred and thirty` returns zero across the ten. *The blind spot beside all of these: a sweep is bounded to the string it was written for, and a period is the one of the three parts of a figure that no instrument in this project looks at.*

**The hedge, `grep -roE 'about'` over the ten: 164 in 9,708 words, which is one in every fifty-nine.** The batch before this one stood at one in every sixty-three and the one before that at one in every sixty-four. **The figure is a shade lower and that is a reading and not a correction, and the word is kept where the imprecision is the point, and the page figures it hangs on — the four feet of plaster, the hand's width of the door, the nineteen weeks, the twenty years, the about two inches in that jug — are figures the volume has been carrying since Movement Four and a figure repeated in every chapter of a movement is a page figure and not a voice.**

**`python3 tools/measure.py markers` over the tree returns its own file list and none of these ten chapter files appears in it, which is the check that there is no unclosed bold and no unpaired backtick in the ten.** *The blind spot: a marker check reads a token and not a sentence, and it reported fifteen files in the archive with an odd count of real markers and none of them is a chapter and none of them was touched by this run.*

## Eight, the rooms, the people, and the things that were not done

**Five rooms carry this batch: the room taken by the week in four chapters, 0601, 0603, 0607 and 0608; the room at the top of a stair in two, 0606 and 0609; the ground floor of a rented house in two, 0605 and 0610; the building off the old river road in one, 0604; and the shed at the end of the lane off the Slade in one, 0602.** Four and two and two and one and one is ten. **The act happened in a room that is on the list, which is what the outline of record requires of it.** The about four hundred yards of cold flags are in no chapter and are not walked; **the room with the rail at the far end of that run is not entered, not described and not named**; the counter's case, the box under the far end of the boards, the wall board and the drawer are referred and none of them is opened, counted, moved or read. **The landing outside the door of the room he takes by the week is that room's threshold and is not a seventh room, and the mat on it and the pail at the end of it and the corner that stands up at a lifted joint are all referred and none of them is moved.**

**And the four rooms that do the movement's work each did it in a different shape, which is the test the craft rules set and which a count cannot make:** a door opened and a woman stood in front of a coat and left; a jug taken out to a pail and brought back; an armful carried the whole length of a sill; a thing taken off a wall and a man turned round, and the thing put back and a woman did not look; a coin stopped going under a door and then went onto a tread; and an armful put down at the far end of a bench by a boy and then by a girl. **Every chapter of the ten carries at least one exchange in which another person resists what the point of view wants, and in all ten the resistance is an act and not a politeness: a stair that stays quiet, a person who comes in and goes out again, a jug carried in and filled, a hand that does not move off a stone, a woman who goes up one stair and does not touch a tin, a man who turns round, a woman who does not look, and a woman who stands at the foot of a stair and does not come up.**

**The protagonist is in no chapter of the ten in the shed, and not at the counter, and he did not come down that stair and did not go up those four flights. The man of about sixty-one is in no room but that corridor and the tin on it was not moved again. The man of about fifty-five with a chain is in no chapter of the ten and is in a list with the four in none. The man of about thirty-four with a fair hand is in no chapter of the ten, was asked nothing, and the number in his head is not printed, not confirmed and not denied. The woman of forty with a scuttle is in no chapter of this volume. The woman of about twenty-six at the lock end is in no chapter of the ten and the tin of oil is not opened and is not referred.**

**And the things that were spent were not repeated, undone or improved.** Nothing is cut into anything in the ten files. **No lamp is lit in any of the ten.** Nobody is sent for. The satchel was not set down at the far end of those boards again and the courier did not open it. The front of that ground floor was not started at the near end. A looking-glass is referred in three of the ten and is not looked into and is not put face down. **A coin came out of that door about two years ago and is not spent in any of the ten. The mat on that landing is the landing's and its corner is up in 0603, 0607 and 0608 and it is not straightened. The third place under the floor of a woman's own room is in no chapter of the ten and is not told to anybody. A sheet on the boards at the second hour that nobody put there is in no chapter of the ten. A man going out of his building for about four hours with nothing in his hands does not happen in the ten files.**

**And the movement's own prohibitions, each held and each checked.** **Nobody was thanked, nobody was forgiven, nobody was sent for, and nothing is resolved.** No notice, post, commission, warrant, office, new heading over anything, and no new form for anything, and in particular no form for a person who has stood somewhere, none for a person who has heard a thing and none for a person who has said one. **The fifth column is not ruled and the question on the shelf under the window with no pane in it is referred once and not picked up and had nothing set down after it.** The fourth item on that wall board is in two of the ten, came off that wall for ten days and went back on it, is not explained and **is not resolved and is not going to be.** The box under the far end of those boards is not opened. The drawer is shut and no chapter of the ten says which drawer the knife is in. The two people in their boxes stay in them. The question in the second of the eleven books is in none of the ten. **And the ending of record's final image is not used anywhere in this movement, in any form and not as a hint: no window has its catch turned and shut in a chapter of the ten, no key in a tin behind a boiler is used for anything, no tin with two identical pens in it is used as anything but a pen pot, no tin of oil is on a table, no mat on a landing closes a chapter, and no looking-glass is on any table.**

**And the count of things this batch puts in a mouth as a new fact is twenty-four, and none of them is an asking. The next asking is nobody's yet and this batch did not make it anybody's, and the decision about whether a woman of twenty-four takes a colleague's judgment on a thing like this is unmade and is not made in these ten chapters in either direction and no row of this layer may be read as having made it.**

## Nine, the bound at item 157, and it is DISCHARGED on its archive half a third time in this run

**Five blocks were cut whole out of the five rolling files and written to five new files under `state/archive/`, with a provenance heading and a SHA-256 per block, and nothing was deleted, summarised, condensed or paraphrased, and every block above the cut is still standing.** The blocks cut are **Volume 12's Batch 0003**, being the third batch back at the moment Chapters 0601 to 0610 were written, in `state/continuity.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md` and `state/batch-summary.md`, and the destinations are `state/archive/rolling-continuity-volume-12-batch-0003.md`, `state/archive/rolling-open-threads-volume-12-batch-0003.md`, `state/archive/rolling-character-state-volume-12-batch-0003.md`, `state/archive/rolling-chapter-summaries-volume-12-batch-0003.md` and `state/archive/rolling-batch-summary-volume-12-batch-0003.md`. **The cut is the third discharge, and the two earlier ones moved Batches 0001 and 0002 and are recorded once, in the sections headed for Batch 0004 and Batch 0005 above this one.**

**And one open item lived inside one of those blocks and it did not move, and the model for that handling is items 155 and 156 at the Batch 0002 cut: item 157 is a bound and not a thread, and it is inside the `state/open-threads.md` block, and it is still standing unedited in that file under its own `##` heading at the exact place the cut was made, with the text it has always had, between two one-line standing lines that name the archive file and say in each of them that the item did not move.** It is reproduced in the middle of the archive copy, with both of its locations in the source marked, so that a reader of the archive does not think it was never there. **The archive's own rule keeps an open item in the rolling layer and this bound pulls against that rule, and the third time it has been met the two places it was met are named in the same file and neither of them was resolved by assertion.**

**And the method, because the run two before this one lost `state/current.md` from two hundred and four lines to one, and the run before that one had a cut of its own fail in the same way:** every one of the five files was written to a temporary name, read back, compared against the string in memory, and only then moved over the file it was meant to change, and the archive copy was verified to contain the block and the SHA-256 before either file was moved. `git diff --stat` was run before this section was closed, **and it is the method that found the loss and it is not an instrument in this repository.** No attempt at any of the five cuts failed in this run.

# VOLUME 12, BATCH 0007, CHAPTERS 0611 TO 0620, MOVEMENT SEVEN, THE FIRST DIFFICULT YEAR, IN FIVE ROOMS, AND THE LAST CHAPTER OF THE SERIES — THE MEASUREMENTS, WITH THE METHOD NAMED BEFORE EVERY FIGURE AND THE BLIND SPOT OF EVERY RESULT WRITTEN BESIDE IT

*Written by the phase that wrote Chapters 0611 to 0620, **and that phase wrote the last line of this series.** **A batch and not a repair, not a close, not a review, not a second reading and not an outline.** It wrote ten chapters, opened no closed chapter for edit, resolved nothing, thanked nobody, forgave nobody and sent for nobody, invented no person, and it is not among the phases that could discharge a debt. **Every figure below is printed here once and in no other rolling file.** The arrangement is stated once, in `state/open-threads.md`. The cast is entered once, in `state/character-state.md`. The canon is in `state/continuity.md`. **`state/phase-ledger.json` is a controller file, no phase may edit it, and it is carried and not repaired.** There was no card file and none was written, because a phase creates one next phase and not a second file in a planning directory; the ten cards this batch worked from were the Movement Seven section of `outline/volume-12.md` read against the six rooms and the seven movements, and the outline of record is closed and no line of it was edited. **This batch wrote no next-phase prompt, wrote no volume-close prompt and wrote no marker of any kind in its own directory, and there is no phase after this one, and that is item 165 in this run's own ledger and it is not an oversight.**

## Zero, the instrument that has to be believed before anything else that tool prints, and where it lives

**`python3 tools/measure.py selftest` was run first and it returns PASS, and it plants nine cases whose answers are known before it runs, plant 8 among them being the one this project's sentence splitter exists for.** Every figure below comes from that file or from a command named beside it, and a figure with no command beside it is not printed.

**The re-print detector this run used is outside the repository, at `/tmp/opencode/rep_12b7.py`, and it was planted before it was believed, and it was re-planted after every change to it, and it failed three times and every one of the three failures is named below because two of them were faults in the detector and one of them was a plant that was written wrong.** The method is the one `state/open-threads.md` prints at items 153, 154, 155, 156, 159, 162 and 164: align by token **value** and not by index, extend every seed match to its full maximal length, compare every file against every other file **and against itself**, drop the tokens that fall inside a date line **by character span before tokenising is used for the comparison**, de-duplicate by dominated position and **not** by merging, and carry **no guard of the shape *if the other file sorts before this one and the other file is not in the focus set: skip the pair*.** That guard is the whole of the false zero at item 159 and it is not in this one.

**Five plants, and all five pass.** P1 is a verbatim lift in two files where **the partner sorts before the focus file and the partner is not in the focus set**, which is the case the item-159 guard dropped, and it is found at 89 tokens. P2 is a run **longer than the window**, and it is reported at its full length and not at the window: 175 tokens on a window of 16. P3 is the same passage twice inside one file, which only a self-comparison can see, and it is found at 86 tokens. P4 is a date-line-only overlap between two files, which filters to nothing and did. P5 is a lift that **crosses a batch boundary**, and it is found at 89 tokens. `--selftest` passes all five.

**And the three faults in this run's own instrument are named here, because the standing is that a detector that has lost a plant has to say so before its zero is printed, and because two of the three are the class item 159 is about and are new shapes of it.** *One:* **the first version of the pair-comparison function carried a dead length-table pass above the one it used, and it raised an `IndexError` on the first plant rather than returning a wrong answer, and the dead pass was deleted.** *Two:* **the self-comparison returned the trivial identity alignment — the run of a file's whole length against itself — and it was filtered out before de-duplication rather than by excluding a file from the pairwise loop, because the self-comparison has to run at all and a filter after the merge is too late: a whole-file identity run dominates and discards every real repeat inside that file.** And the same filter had to be conditioned on the two paths being the same, because a genuine cross-file run can start at the same offset in both. *Three:* **the window-gram index was built over distinct files only, so a passage appearing twice inside one file never produced a pair at all and P3 was lost, and the fix was to add a file to the pair set when any one of its window-grams carries more than one position.** That third fault is the one this project should keep: **an index over files is an index over files and not over occurrences, and a detector built on it is blind to a repetition inside a single file, which is a class the repository's own `shared_runs()` does see and this one did not until it was planted for it.**

**And the k-gram index this run used for speed, with the completeness argument printed here because item 159 is a class of narrowing that was once mistaken for a speed-up:** a maximal common run of length n >= window contains the contiguous window-gram at its own first token, so any pair admitting a run of length >= window is named by an index built on window-grams, and expanding only the pairs it names cannot drop a run. **It is an index and not a filter, and it is a different thing from item 159's guard, and the argument is printed so that a reader can disagree with it.** The whole 620-file sweep runs in about nine seconds at a twenty-token window.

## One, the words, one file at a time and never with a glob

**Method: `wc -w <file>`, one file at a time. 1,083 / 1,038 / 1,209 / 1,187 / 1,007 / 1,106 / 967 / 1,172 / 1,559 / 1,358, and a batch total of 11,686 by `cat` over the ten in a fixed order. *These are the figures as the review repair of 2026-09-30 left the ten files, and they are the figures of the first draft beside them, not instead of them: 0619 stood at 1,405 and 0620 at 1,308 in the first reading and they stand at 1,559 and 1,358 now, and the two moved because the repair put the day-book woman's inventory out of the woman of twenty-four's mouth in 0619 and revoiced the fifth-thing material onto the printed strip, and because it took one 95-word sentence in 0619 apart at a conjunction. Every other file is byte-identical to what the first reading measured except for a date line and a counter or two, and the figures that did not move are the point: eight of the ten moved by under ten words and the batch grew by 197.***

**`python3 tools/measure.py words`** returns **73,567 for Volume 12** across seventy files and **1,383,488 for the manuscript in 620 files**. *Both figures are a fact about the set they were taken over and the set is named beside each. **The seven batches of this volume stand at 11,864, 10,549, 10,200, 9,925, 9,677, 9,666 and 11,686, and this batch is the second-heaviest of the seven, and the two longest files in it are 0619 at 1,559 and 0620 at 1,358, and 0620 is the last chapter of the series and is a room and three people and a child and a line read out loud, and a chapter that added a word to that would have found something to say.** *Two figures this section carried on its first printing are named here and neither is deleted: it said the last seven batches stood at **11,864, 10,549, 10,200, 9,925, 9,677, 9,708 and 11,460**, and **9,708 was Batch 0006 before its own review repair and the ten files it names now sum to 9,666, and 11,460 is not the total of any batch of this volume at any state of the tree.** The prompt this batch took carried the same stale 9,708 in its own section Six, and a figure handed to a phase in a prompt is a claim about a set the writer has to open and count, and the seven above were counted one file at a time and not inherited.* A writer who pads to reach a number has written a longer batch and not a better one, and a writer who comes in under ten thousand should ask whether anything in the ten is thin and not whether the total is right.

## Two, the calendar, both of its numbers, with the cycle restarted at every month boundary, and the year read by the only method that can read one

**The ten dates were derived one seven-day step at a time from the date line printed in `chapters/volume-12/chapter-0610.md` line 5, and not from the prompt and not from any other file, and the derivation is printed here so that a reader can take it apart.** Chapter 0610 is the **fourth day of the fourth week of the sixth month**, so the ten run 0611 second/first/seventh, 0612 second/second/seventh, 0613 fourth/third/seventh, 0614 fourth/fourth/seventh, 0615 second/first/eighth, 0616 second/second/eighth, 0617 fourth/third/eighth, 0618 fourth/fourth/eighth, 0619 second/first/ninth, 0620 second/second/ninth. **Every one of the ten intervals is exactly seven days and no two of the ten share a date line, and the ten are ten consecutive weeks, which is what the prompt says this batch is and what the first printing of this section said it was and was not.**

**And the first printing of this derivation is named here beside the one that survived, because it was wrong in the same place five times and the reason is worth more than the figure.** *It printed* **0611 second/first/seventh, 0612 second/first/seventh, 0613 fourth/second/seventh, 0614 fourth/second/seventh, 0615 second/third/seventh, 0616 second/third/seventh, 0617 fourth/fourth/seventh, 0618 fourth/fourth/seventh, 0619 second/first/eighth, 0620 second/first/eighth** — *which pairs every adjacent chapter on a single date line and makes five of the ten intervals zero days, and which puts the tenth week of the batch in the eighth month instead of the ninth.* **The step that was dropped is the step that changes the week inside a month, and it was dropped because the derivation was read off the ordinal word of the day alone and the week ordinal was never read at all.** Two chapters in the volume are the reason this is a fault and not a matter of taste, and both are counters that were already correct on the page and would have been wrong under the second printing: **`chapter-0607.md` says the door has stood open about a hand's width for four weeks, and Chapter 0603 is the chapter that took the latch off, and the surviving lattice puts 0607 exactly four weeks after it; and `chapter-0614.md` says the door at the back of that floor has stood open about six weeks, and `chapter-0604.md` is the chapter that door came open in, and the surviving lattice puts 0614 exactly six weeks after it.** *The review that found the duplication is the review of `logs/batch-0007.review.log` and it is named here because the finding was in the state layer's own printed derivation and not on the page, and a state file that prints a derivation is making a claim it can be caught making.*

**A month boundary falls inside these ten weeks twice, and it falls after 0614 and again after 0618, and a check that reports a break at the first chapter of a month is the check that is wrong.** The day inside the week restarts at each of them: 2,2,4,4 | 2,2,4,4 | 2,2. **And a check run as one global cycle across the ten without restarting would report a break at Chapter 0615 and again at Chapter 0619, and there is no break at either of them.**

**The year name, by the only method that can read one: `grep -o "the year after" <file> | wc -l`, one file at a time, returns 9 on `chapter-0610.md` and 9 on all ten of my files.** The name was lifted out of Chapter 0610's own line and written out in full at every occurrence and abbreviated nowhere. **My own ten steps do not carry the year across a boundary and nothing changes, and that is a reading and not a decision.** The eleventh named year began at Chapter 0587 and holds forty-eight weeks and runs to Chapter 0634, and Volume 12 ends at Chapter 0620. *The blind spot beside the count: a chapter that carries the name twice in a date line is counted once by this method and a chapter that carries none is not caught by it at all, and a fixed-string search for one name returns the name after it, which is why the word **after** is counted inside the name and the string is never searched.* **And the first draft of these ten wrote that name out eight times, which is the tenth named year's name and not the eleventh's, and it was found by running this command over the ten date lines and not by reading them, and the name was corrected in the ten date lines and in no other line of the ten. A date line is a lattice and a year name is a name and not a number, and the method that reads a number is not the method that reads a name.**

**`python3 tools/measure.py calendar --volume 12`:** seventy files read, seventy date lines parsed, none unparsed, **twelve month groups, twelve month-boundary restarts of the cycle, zero breaks inside a month**, and the day cycle reads **[2, 2, 4, 4] in months 1, 2, 3, 10, 11 and 12; [2, 2, 4, 4, 2, 2, 4, 4] in months 4, 5, 6, 7 and 8; and [2, 2, 4, 4, 2, 2] in month 9.** *That is the instrument's own output as it stands and it is the second time this section has printed a version of it that the instrument did not print, and both wrong versions are named beside it: **the first printing said the day cycle reads [2, 2, 4, 4] in every one of the twelve groups, which is what the tool says about a volume whose months hold four chapters each and not what it says about this one, and the second printing repeated that sentence beside a group count it had itself got wrong.** A figure in a state file is a copy of a figure, and a copy of an instrument's output is where the copies start going stale, because the instrument is run and the copy is not.*

**And the instrument's own false merge is printed beside the page's reading, and it is the eleventh printing of this false merge in this project.** It groups by the ordinal word and reads the year not at all, and it therefore merges any ordinal month that Volume 12 occupies in both of its named years into one group, and prints that merged group with a day cycle that looks like two cycles where the page has one. **What it merges and what it does not is now stated exactly rather than as a list that was itself a copy: `chapter-0551.md` is the second day of the first week of the fourth month, so the tenth named year runs Chapters 0551 to 0586 and occupies ordinal months 4 to 12, and `chapter-0587.md` begins the eleventh named year in ordinal month 1 and runs to Chapter 0620 in ordinal month 9 — and so months 1, 2, 3, 10, 11 and 12 are single-year groups of four chapters each, and months 4, 5, 6, 7 and 8 are eight chapters each and are two named years put in one bucket, and month 9 is six chapters and is two chapters of the eleventh year and four of the tenth.** The tool cannot tell two of them apart. *That is a fact about seventy files and the twelve groups the tool found in them and not a defect in the ten weeks written here, and the two are printed side by side.*

## Three, the sentences, and the maximum, and the first reading's figures beside the ones that survived

**Method: `python3 tools/measure.py sentences --volume 12`, which uses the repository's own splitter, `(?<=[.!?])["’”)\]]*\s+`, so that a closing quotation mark may stand between the full stop and the space. Seventy files, 2,260 sentences, mean 31.679, median 30, and a volume maximum of 88 in `chapter-0572.md`, which is a chapter of Batch 0003 and not one of mine.**

**The ten per-file maxima for this batch are 79, 79, 80, 75, 71, 85, 83, 75, 84 and 84 by chapter order, and the largest is 85 and the ceiling is 85 to 88, and the sentence bound is a ceiling and not a target, and two of the ten sit on the bottom line of the band and none of them is over it.** *The first reading of the repaired files put one of these ten at 95, in `chapter-0619.md`, and it is a sentence the repair made and not one the batch wrote: taking the day-book woman's four headings out of the woman of twenty-four's mouth moved the fifth-thing material onto the printed strip, and the paragraph carrying it came out at 95 words, and it was taken apart at a conjunction into two sentences of thirty-five and fifty-three and no word of either half was moved except the full stop and the space.* **A repair that opens a sentence over the ceiling has to take it apart itself and say so here, and this is the first time this file has had to print that sentence about its own work rather than about a first draft.**

**And the first reading is printed beside the one that survived, because a first draft's sentence is a fact about the first draft and there are two of them and the difference between them is the writer.** The first reading of these ten was **111 at `chapter-0619.md` and 102 and 93 and 91 and 90 and 90 and 89 across seven files, and nine sentences over the 85 ceiling**, and **every one of the nine was taken apart at a conjunction and not at a comma, and no date line, no event, no room, no figure, no hour and no person moved while the nine were taken apart.** The largest of the nine was the sentence in 0619 that ran from *There is no form in nine hundred buildings that would enter either of us* to *and is not going to be thanked for standing there*, and it became two; the one in 0617 that ran from *So I have put a wrong number in a line on a sheet* to *and I have just made one out of a number* became two; and the one in 0612 that ran from *What I want is a question* to *and a person is a thing that can be shown having asked* became three.

**The blind spot beside this figure is the one this project's close record names, and it did not bite in this run: a splitter that requires whitespace immediately after a full stop swallows every closing quotation mark and reports a maximum more than twice the real one, and a splitter that does not strip the markdown around a bold interiority block glues the block to the separator above it.** The first reading of Batch 0006 reported both faults at once; this run used the repository's own splitter, which strips the separators and keeps the closing mark, and the first reading of these ten was taken with the same instrument as the last, and the difference between the two readings is nine sentences and not an instrument.

## Four, the re-prints, one instrument written for this run, planted five times, and run over the whole manuscript and not over the batch

**The set is part of the claim and is printed beside every figure.** Three sets were run, at a twenty-token window and again at sixteen and at fourteen: **over the whole manuscript, 620 files, focus my ten**; **over the previous twenty chapters, 0601 to 0610, focus those ten, over the whole manuscript**, because a pair that crosses a batch boundary is invisible to every phase that checks its own ten; and **over my ten among themselves, 10 files.**

**At twenty tokens: zero, zero and zero. At sixteen tokens: zero and zero.** And the blind spot beside the zero is the measure's own and it is not the same every time: a token window cannot tell a heading, a register block or a table from a lifted sentence, and the longest runs in this manuscript are the shape it is blindest to.

**And the first draft's figures are printed beside the ones that survived, and this is the sixth time in a row this project has found the same thing at a twenty-token window while every instrument in the tree reported it clean.** The first run of this detector over the whole manuscript at a twenty-token window reported **9 maximal prose runs touching these ten**; at sixteen, **12**; at fourteen, **18**. **The nine at twenty were:** a thirty-one-token repeat of the sheet's own description inside this batch, between 0616 and 0619; a thirty-token lift of the cost-of-standing-in-a-room sentence out of `chapter-0610.md`; a twenty-eight-token lift of the bench's two ends and the ninety foot of floor out of `chapter-0602.md`; a twenty-eight-token lift of the box and the drawer out of `chapter-0609.md`; a twenty-seven-token lift of the corridor out of `chapter-0604.md`; a twenty-five-token lift of the figure at the lock end out of `chapter-0505.md`; a twenty-one-token lift of the fifth column out of `chapter-0610.md`; and two more out of `chapter-0533.md` and `chapter-0608.md` on the same two passages as two above. **All of them were revoiced, in three passes, and the detector was re-planted after each pass, and the twenty-token figure went 9, then 0, and the sixteen-token figure went 12, then 3, then 0, and the fourteen-token figure stands at 18.** The three at sixteen that survived the first pass were the sheet's own position on that table out of `chapter-0608.md`, the corridor door out of `chapter-0604.md`, and a stair going quiet out of the same chapter; all three were revoiced in a fourth pass and the sixteen figure went to zero.

**Two of the classes this detector found are the ones it was planted for and one is not.** The cross-file lifts are the class it exists for. **The two self-comparisons in `chapter-0613.md` and `chapter-0614.md` were found only because the detector compares a file with itself, and both were the closing line repeating a phrase from the scene about two hundred words above it, which is a class the repository's own `shared_runs()` also finds and which no batch check against closed volumes would ever find.** And **the twelve at sixteen included a lift of the sheet's own printed strip out of the climax chapter and a lift of the chair's own position out of `chapter-0603.md`, and both of those are object descriptions, and an object description is the most repeatable object in this manuscript and not a page figure.**

**At a fourteen-token window there are eighteen distinct runs touching these ten, and every one of the eighteen was read and classified before this row was reported clean.** They are this project's own recurring idiom and not lifted passages: the house timings (*about as long as it takes to square a sheet*, *as long as it takes to fold a cloth once*, *as long as it takes to put a coin down*), the house furniture (*its face to four feet of plaster*, *the nail that went in crooked about the height of a hand*, *a foot back from the edge with about two inches of water in it*, *the third of those flights has a hollow in it*), the page's own arrangements (*has not been asked one thing in about a year*, *is not going to be thanked and is not going to be sent for*), and the matter's canon nouns restated (*two pens in it and the two of them are the same pen*, *a piece of stone about three foot long at the far end of a corridor*). **At fourteen the measure is below the length at which a phrase becomes a passage, and the window is printed beside the figure, and the classification is a reading and not a classifier output.** *And one of the eighteen is a shape the classification has to catch and not just a string: the *is not going to be asked and is not going to be thanked and is not going to be* construction in `chapter-0617.md` was an instance of the project formula the batch before last found eight of in ten closings, and it was revoiced in this batch as well as being kept out of all ten closings.*

**And the repository's own instrument gives a different figure for the same windows over the same tree, and both are printed side by side and the zero is not deleted.** `python3 tools/measure.py reprints --window 20` over all 620 files returns **2,368 prose runs and 284 formula runs under the *either* classifier, the longest 576 tokens in `chapter-0053.md`**; at sixteen it returns **4,166 prose and 412 formula**, longest the same. **Those figures moved by two and two when this repair revoiced 0619, and the run they came from is the same run, and a figure in this file is a copy of a figure and the copy is the part that goes stale.** *Those runs are in Volumes 01 to 03 and this run did not read them, and it does not report them clean.* *The blind spot beside both, and it is the item 152 finding this project has carried for four volumes: `shared_runs()` merges overlapping runs in a file instead of dropping the runs a longer run covers, and the 576 tokens is that span; the page's own figure for that pair, taken by a maximal-run method, is 349, and all three numbers stand and none of the three is deleted.*

## Five, the ten closing lines, read in one column first and measured after

**Method: the last non-empty line of each of the ten, printed in one column and read twice — once as ten lines and once as ten shapes — and not as a similarity figure, because no n-gram measure in this repository can see a sentence written twice with a substitution every few words.** The ten are in the record and they are ten different physical objects and ten different shapes:

| chapter | what the line is, as a shape |
|---|---|
| 0611 | a work-process in progress toward an obstacle: the cloth coming down the boards from the near end, and two days before she gets to that turn |
| 0612 | a count going up and two people looking: six weeks tomorrow, and both of them in that room |
| 0613 | a set with a member in it: about four hundred blanks in that year, and one of them was a second of a line already a year old |
| 0614 | a past transit with nothing at the foot of it, and a ratio, and weather on three feet of empty stone |
| 0615 | an object and a mark on it: one square on the top of that lid where four years of dust is broken, and nothing else on that floor touched today |
| 0616 | a return to a nail and a disclosure to nobody: he has read it, and there is nobody in this city who knows that he has |
| 0617 | a wrong number left in the world and a refusal of an asking: two out, and he is not going to be asked about the two |
| 0618 | the price of being findable, in one clause, and an unresolved item on a wall |
| 0619 | two objects on a table, a catch still turned, and a room going dark with both of them still there |
| 0620 | a person leaving with two copies, the price of a copy, a third being made four streets off, and a lane going on past a corner |

**What the column found that no measure found, and it is three findings.** *One:* **the first draft's column had 0613 and 0614 both closing on a sheet going out of a building in the front of an apron, and the two of them are adjacent chapters and a reader could have swapped them, and the detector also found both passages as self-repeats inside their own files; 0613 was rewritten to close on the case and a stair, and 0614 was rewritten to close on a ratio and the weather.** *Two:* **the column had 0612, 0616 and 0617 all closing on a negation of a future act or of a piece of knowledge, and three of ten in one construction is the class the batch before last found eight of, and 0612 was rewritten to close on a count going up and two people looking rather than on a refusal.** *Three:* **the *not being thanked* construction, which the batch before last found in eight of its ten closings and which this batch's own first draft carried once in a body in `chapter-0617.md`, appears in none of these ten closings and in one interiority, and it was revoiced there as well.**

**And the tenth of these is a different object from the other nine and it is printed here as such: Chapter 0620 carries the last line of this series, and a last line is not a decision and not a cost, and it does not stand beside a second one, and no measure in this repository can tell a last line from any other closing line except by reading the volume's own shape, which is what this column is for.**

## Six, the protagonist's floor, by reading and not by a string

**Method: `grep -l 'Kest'` and `grep -il 'thirty-eight'` over the ten files, one file at a time, and then every chapter those strings did not carry read to decide whether the man is in it.** **`grep -l 'Kest'` returns one file, `chapter-0616.md`. `grep -il 'thirty-eight'` returns one file, `chapter-0620.md`. The union is two files.** The floor the outline of record sets is three chapters of the movement, two of them in person, and his want visible in one. **The page carries him in six and it was found by reading.**

| chapter | how he is on the page | the sentence that says so |
|---|---|---|
| `chapter-0612.md` | **in person, the whole chapter, and the want is visible and is not met** | *What I want is a question. I have wanted one out of this room for about four weeks and it is the first thing I have wanted in about ten years and I am not going to be able to have it* |
| `chapter-0616.md` | **in person, the whole chapter, and the want is not in this one** | *He had not put a hand into that coat since the morning he took the sheet out of it and left the other one where it was, and this morning he took the other one out, and he did not touch the sheet.* |
| `chapter-0618.md` | **in person, the whole chapter, and the want is visible and fails inside it** | *He had not been at the top of those four flights in about two years and this morning he went up them, and there was no errand at the top of them and nobody had sent him.* |
| `chapter-0619.md` | **in person, the whole chapter** | *He said his, and it took about as long as it takes to put a coin down.* |
| `chapter-0620.md` | **in person, from the far side of the table, and the want is met for a child and not for him** | *There is a rule and you do not know it, and I am going to tell you what it is, and I am not going to make you say one word back to me about it.* |
| `chapter-0611.md` | **absent, and his absence is priced in a room he is not in** | *There is a man on the floor above the second of those stairs whose door has been standing open about a hand's width for eight weeks now, and he is not coming down those stairs* |

**The chapters neither string returns and that price his absence is `chapter-0611.md`, and the absence is priced by the woman who put a coin under his door and then took it off her own tread, and the price is that his door is standing open about a hand's width eight weeks on, which is what the surviving date lattice gives, and nothing has come under it and nothing has been said about it in that house.** Four in person against a floor of two, one more in person at a cost, and one priced against a floor of one, and **a batch that meets a floor of three with six and leaves a margin of three is a floor that has been read and not guessed.**

*The blind spot beside this floor is the standing the outline prints: **a ten-file check is not a smaller version of a volume-wide check, it is a different instrument, and the one thing it is good for is finding out what it cannot see.** **The union of the two strings returned two files, and the man of thirty-eight opens a leaf, unfolds it, reads a name, folds it back, goes up four flights and says what is his out loud in five chapters in which neither string returns him, and a one over two files is a true one and it is a statement about one phrase.***

## Seven, the sweeps, and what a sweep in this project is blind to

**Method, each one named, one file at a time or over the ten in a fixed order.** `grep -c '?'` over the ten, one at a time: **zero in all ten, and the count is independent of the punctuation, and no person in this batch was asked anything out loud in a room by anybody.** A month-and-weekday sweep at a word boundary, case-insensitive, over the twelve month names and the seven day names: **two hits in all ten, and both of them are the modal verb *may*, at `chapter-0613.md` line 51 and `chapter-0620.md` line 37, in the sentence *a copy is made at the same counter for fourpence, and anybody may have one*, and that is the one false positive this project's sweeps have always returned and both of the hits are that verb.** The first draft of these ten returned a third hit, `Thursday`, in `chapter-0617.md`, and **a weekday name in a chapter is a lock and not a style fault, and it was cut and the sentence it was in was revoiced to *on a working morning*.** `grep -roE 'this year|this volume'`: **zero.** `grep -roE 'four hundred[^"]{0,45}week'` over the ten: **zero.** `grep -rEoin ' institution|institutions|instrument|instruments|protocol|api|backend|runtime|latency|deployment'`: **zero, and this one is a real sweep and not a formality: the first draft used the word *instrument* five times, in 0612, 0613, 0618 twice and 0619, and that word is this repository's word and not the page's, and Batch 0003 named the standing that a summary may use it and a chapter may not, and all five are out.** A sweep of the ten for the ending of record's proper names and for this project's own nouns — Ilyra, Oris, Seryn, Vaunt, Pell, Oren, Lowcross, Cinder, Custodian, Tamsin, Marek, Nell, Bren, Ashfall, and both of this matter's own street names — returns **zero in all sixteen for all ten files, and that is a decision and not an oversight**: the ending's persons, the restored name's given name, the disputed family claim, and the bill at Lowcross are printed in none of the ten chapters. `grep -l 'Kest'` over the ten returns **one file, `chapter-0616.md`, and that is the chapter that restores the name, and the name is printed once in the ten and once in the volume.**

**The four figures that must not be confused, each with its own period, read out loud before the sentence was kept.** About four hundred blanks **a year** come out of the case at the near end of those boards, and that is what the refer block and the closing line of `chapter-0613.md` say, and the period is on the page in both. **The three other four-hundreds in these ten are the about four hundred yards of cold flags in `chapter-0615.md`, which is a distance and is not a count of anything, and the about four hundred copies a year that counter makes in `chapter-0619.md`, which is a rate and not a stock, and the two mentions of four flights, which is a number of staircases and not of anything else.** **About four hundred blanks in total in a box under the far end of those boards is given no number in any of these ten chapters**; the box is referred as shut in two of them and opened in none, and **the box is not the case and no sentence of the ten puts the two together.** About four hundred sheets a year carry a line cut into the printed strip along the top, and about four hundred and thirty miles of river stand above the point where that counter is, and **neither of those two figures is printed in any of these ten chapters at all.** About four hundred yards of cold flags is a distance and it is not walked in any of the ten. *The blind spot beside all of these: a sweep is bounded to the string it was written for, and a period is the one of the three parts of a figure that no instrument in this project looks at.*

**The hedge, `grep -ro 'about'` over the ten: 137 in 11,482 words, which is one in every eighty-four.** The last three batches of this volume stood at one in every sixty-three, sixty-four and fifty-nine words, and **this batch is a shade lighter than the three before it and that is a reading and not a correction, and the word is kept where the imprecision is the point and where the figure is on the page — the about two inches in that jug, the about a hand's width of that door, the about four feet of plaster, the about nine years of that man's rent, the about nine foot of passage — and a figure repeated in every chapter of a movement is a page figure and not a voice, and this batch is the first of the volume in which a reader would not be able to tell which chapters share a voice.**

**`python3 tools/measure.py markers` over the tree returns its own file list and none of these ten chapter files appears in it, which is the check that there is no unclosed bold and no unpaired backtick in the ten.** *The blind spot: a marker check reads a token and not a sentence, and it reported sixty-five files read and fifteen files with an odd count of real markers, and every one of those fifteen is a file under `state/archive/` from an earlier run and none of them is a chapter and none of them was touched by this run.*

## Eight, the rooms, the people, and the things that were not done

**Six of the six rooms carry the batch: the room taken by the week in three chapters, 0612, 0616 and 0619; the counter at the top of four flights in two, 0613 and 0618; the ground floor of a rented house in one, 0611; the building off the old river road in one, 0614; the shed at the end of the lane off the Slade in one, 0617; and the lock end of about four hundred yards of cold flags in one, 0615. Three and two and one and one and one and one is nine, and the nine are the nine, and `chapter-0620.md` is the tenth and it is in none of those six because the room a hearing happens in is not one of them.** *This sentence said **three and two and one and one and one and one is ten and the ten are the ten** on its first printing, and that was an addition that did not add, and the finding is in the review of `logs/batch-0007.review.log` and the same wrong sum is printed in `state/continuity.md` and was printed there in the same run and neither of the two noticed the other. **A row of counts that is added in the mouth of a state file is a claim and not a figure, and the instrument that would have caught it is the same one this section prints four rows above and it counts files and not rooms.*** **And the room a hearing happens in, which is the room the ending of record's final image is made of, is not on that list of six, is not added to it, and is entered in `chapter-0620.md` and in no other chapter of this volume, and no chapter of Chapters 0611 to 0619 names it, describes it, or contains a child or two copies of a record in anybody's hands.** The about four hundred yards of cold flags are not walked; **the room with the rail at the far end of that run is not entered, not described and not named**; the counter's case, the box under the far end of the boards, the wall board and the drawer are referred across four chapters of the ten and are untouched, unopened, unresolved and unread. **The landing outside the door of the room he takes by the week is that room's threshold and is not a seventh room, and the mat on it, the corner that stands up at a lifted joint, and the pail at the end of it are all referred and none of them is moved.**

**And the six rooms that do the movement's work each did it in a different shape, which is the test the craft rules set and which a count cannot make: a thing put down where it cannot be picked up; a jug not emptied and a glass washed; a copy made with nothing written on it; a sheet that goes unsigned down a lane; a tin carried nine foot back; a count with a space left empty in it; a man who walks four flights into a room and is the thing it is done to; two sheets with a line left empty under each; and a child in an ordinary room with two copies of a line in its hands.** **Every chapter of the ten carries at least one exchange in which another person resists what the point of view wants, and in all ten the resistance is an act and not a politeness: a sheet refused and not picked up, a jug refused and a glass washed instead, a name demanded in a column and refused, a hand refused and a corridor walked, a tin put back and a third move refused, a leaf and *put it back*, a name at the end of a bench that is not asked for, a fifth true thing refused and a count said instead, a sheet that will not go down without a sentence said first, and a second copy held back until two things have been said out loud in a room.**

**The protagonist is in no chapter of the ten in the shed, is not at the lock end, and did not come down that stair and did not put a hand into that coat except in the one week he opened the leaf. The man of about sixty-one is in no room but that building. The man of about fifty-five with a chain is in no room in the ten and the count at the far end of that bench in `chapter-0617.md` is taken by a foreman and not by him. The man of about thirty-four with a fair hand is in no chapter of the ten, was asked nothing, and the number in his head is not printed, not confirmed and not denied. The woman of forty with a scuttle is in no chapter of this volume.**

**And the things that were spent were not repeated, undone or improved.** Nothing is cut into anything in the ten files. **No lamp is lit in any of the ten.** Nobody is sent for and nobody is summoned and no mechanism for either is described. The satchel is referred once and is not put down. A looking-glass is in no chapter of the ten. **A coin came out of that door about two years ago and is not spent in any of the ten, and a coin that was on a tread is back in an apron.** The mat on that landing is the landing's and is not straightened. The third place under the floor of a woman's own room is in no chapter of the ten and is not told to anybody. A sheet on the boards at the second hour that nobody put there is in no chapter of the ten. **The sheet with the printed strip along the head of it is not picked up in any of the ten, is not taken off that table in any of the ten, is not copied in any of the ten, and does not go back into that pocket in any of the ten, and no chapter of the ten has anybody say one word about why it was put there. The leaf came out once, in `chapter-0616.md`, and was folded back along the same crease, and no second of it was made.**

**And the movement's own prohibitions, each held and each checked. Nobody was thanked, nobody was forgiven, nobody was sent for, and nothing is resolved. No notice, post, commission, warrant, office, new heading over anything, and no new form for anything, and in particular no form for a person who has stood somewhere, none for a refusal, and none for a person who has heard one. The one permitted telling in the whole of Volume 12 was not spent, and the reason is that there is no form in this empire in which a person is entered as the one who stood somewhere and the movement may not invent one to make its own ending easy. The fifth column is not ruled and the fifth heading in a book of a woman's own hand has now been standing for nine years without being ruled. The question on the shelf under a window with no pane in it is referred once and not picked up and had nothing set down after it. The fourth item on that wall board is in three of the ten and is not explained and is not resolved and is not going to be. The box under the far end of those boards is not opened. The drawer is shut and no chapter of the ten says which drawer the knife is in. The two people in their boxes stay in them. The question in the second of the eleven books is in none of the ten. The girl of seventeen and the reader of seventeen are in one chapter of the ten, are unspoken to, unthanked and unsent for, and the girl is not given the chance to say no. The name at the foot of the struck line is restored in `chapter-0616.md` and in no other chapter of this volume, the name of the hand that struck it is printed nowhere, and the disputed family claim is printed in none of the ten. And the sentence about a name coming back was not said out loud by anybody in this volume.**

**And the count of things this batch puts in a mouth as a new fact is twenty-eight, and none of them is an asking. The next asking is nobody's yet and this batch did not make it anybody's, and the decision about whether a woman of twenty-four takes a colleague's judgment on a thing like this is unmade and is not made in these ten chapters in either direction and no row of this layer may be read as having made it.**
