### Five: what was checked and held, with the command beside each figure

`python3 tools/measure.py selftest` before anything else and again at the end: **PASS on all eleven plants, two of which are the `lifts` command.** `python3 tools/measure.py calendar --volume 11`: **fifty files read, fifty date lines parsed, twelve month-boundary restarts, one break inside a month in month 3 with days [4, 4, 2, 2, 4, 4], unparsed none** — identical before and after, which is the check that matters most here, because Volume 11's calendar runs into Volume 12 without a gap.

**Date lines: all ten byte-identical to `694f0b4` and on the line numbers they held there, which are 9 9 11 11 11 9 11 9 7 9.** The locator was the whole calendar formula and nothing less, and it returned **exactly one** line in each of the ten:

```
grep -n "^It is the .* day of the .* week of the .* month of the year after" chapters/volume-11/chapter-0$n.md
```

**`^It is ` matches two lines in `chapter-0523.md` and in `chapter-0526.md` on this range and `^It is the ` still matches two in `chapter-0523.md`, and both failure modes are the ones item Nine-Four set.** **The check was also run on line 5 as printed and reports SAME on all ten**, and this phase took no paragraph out of or into any position above a date line, which is the failure the tenth and eleventh ranges walked into in three files between them. **It is worth saying that the one line this phase's own audit found, the added paragraph in `chapter-0530.md` that opens `It is nine foot by eleven`, is invisible to every one of these checks** — a line-level diff reports it as an ordinary insertion, the calendar reads the file's real date line, and only the strip inside the measurement helper notices it. **That is section Two again and it is the reason that section is here.**

**Titles: all ten byte-identical on line 1.** **Dialogue: ten files IDENTICAL and zero CHANGED**, checked by md5 over every span between quotation marks, with span counts 8 8 5 1 5 6 6 10 5 3 — **and that claim is made because the command was run, which is the standing item 173J set after a record once made it without running anything.** **Section breaks: 6 5 7 5 6 5 6 6 5 5 on both sides of every file**, measured rather than taken from the prompt's line, and they agree with it. **Bold markers: 2 4 0 2 2 4 2 0 4 4 raw `**` in every file and every count even**; `chapter-0523.md` and `chapter-0528.md` have none and this phase added no interiority to a chapter that had none. `python3 tools/measure.py markers` reports its odd-marker files in `state/archive/` and **none in this range**. Question marks **zero**. `this year`, `this volume`, `this novel` **zero**. Month and weekday names **zero** — not even the modal *may*, which means the guard ran and found nothing. **Trailing newline present in all ten**, and the check for it printed nothing.

**The diff, run after every editing pass and not once at the end.** Word-level, each file against `694f0b4`: **3,788 insertions, zero deletions, zero substitutions.** Line-level over the same files: **90 insertions, 670 equal, zero deletions, zero `replace` opcodes.** **The line-level figure is the stronger statement and it is the one that holds: nothing was ever appended to an original line, no added paragraph sits inside an original paragraph, and this audit — which rewrote nine added paragraphs in full and then had six of them corrected by its review — still reports zero `replace` opcodes, because every one of those paragraphs was itself an insertion and an insertion can be replaced without touching the original. Both figures are unchanged by the review's corrections except in the word column, and that is the check on the review itself: six added paragraphs came back to full and no original line moved.**

**The import indices, all three, with the baseline beside the added figure because the added figure on its own means nothing in this manuscript.** `python3 tools/measure.py lifts --volume 11 --first 521 --last 530 --base 694f0b4 --min 6 --show 20`: **ADDED 45 prose lines, 41 over six words, 1 over nine words, mean 7.05; BASELINE 259 prose lines, 238 over six, 167 over nine, mean 10.58.** **The added prose is cleaner than this book's own baseline on every measure and it is the same figure the audit published before the review's corrections, to the digit, which is the check on those corrections: putting canon back did not put an import back with it.** The one run at nine words or more is *in a room at the top of a stair* in `chapter-0530.md`, held by `chapter-0543.md`, and **it is reported and not removed, which is the decision the last four ranges have each come to on their own remaining runs.** **Ten eight-word runs stand and not twelve, and every one was read: the register's stock connectives, one fixture clause, and the one clause of the volume's own about a stair in `chapter-0524.md` that no rewriting of it improved — which is the clause the review found was still duplicated, and which now says something different from its copy in `chapter-0530.md`.** Added against added, across the range's own ten chapters, at eight words: zero runs, down from two. Added against the ORIGINAL text of the range's own chapters, at eight words: zero runs, down from two** — those four were findings one, four, seven and eight above, and **`lifts` saw none of them.**

**Re-prints, all four windows and all three classifiers, on Volume 11 and with the tool named.** `python3 tools/measure.py reprints --window NN --volume 11`, `either` classifier, prose bucket: **4 / 494 / 1,733 / 4,305** at the twenty-, twelve-, eight- and five-word windows, against **4 / 494 / 1,699 / 3,784** at `4be7315` in a scratch tree as the prompt records it. **The twenty-word figure is unmoved at 4, which is the only window that can catch a long import, and all four of its runs are original prose:** the twenty-nine-word run is *A name stood once at the foot of a leaf…* in `chapter-0530.md` against `chapter-0539.md`, and `chapter-0530.md`'s copy is **original**, verified against `694f0b4` and not inherited from the range's record, which claims the same and is right. The twenty-word run is *not being asked and she let that go in a month that is gone…* in `chapter-0525.md` against `chapter-0534.md`, and `chapter-0525.md`'s copy is **original**. **The eight-word figure came down by two and the twelve- and five-word figures are within four of where the audit left them, on forty-seven words of corrected prose; a reader who wants the long-import answer has the twenty-word figure and it did not move.** The `date` and `terms` buckets are 4 / 494 / 1,740 / 4,358 and 4 / 499 / 1,747 / 4,327. **The runs by which `terms` exceeds `either` are date lines, and the seventy-four-word `terms` run in `chapter-0529.md` is its date line, which puts the phrase *the year after* twice in one clause — a property of the formula and not a defect in the prose.** `date` catches all fifty. **The tool prints three numbers per window and the figure this record carries is the prose bucket of the third; the middle one, the total, is 54 at the twenty-word window on Volume 11, and fifty of those are the fifty date lines. A record that quotes the prose bucket has to say so, and this one now does.**

**The closing-section person, on the scan printed in the section headed *THE PROSE REPAIR OF CHAPTERS 0601 TO 0609*, used as printed and not rebuilt, with `694f0b4` and this range's file list: zero mismatches, all ten THIRD before and after.** The bold interiority and the dialogue lines were subtracted before anything was counted. **The scan was planted six ways before its answer was believed and all six behave as the record above claims: a first-person closing section returns FIRST, a third-person closing section returns THIRD, a bold interiority returns THIRD, a dialogue-only closing section returns THIRD, a first-person section followed by a third-person closing section returns THIRD, and a line with the speech set into a sentence returns FIRST** — the plant that does not behave, because the printed filter strips a line that starts or ends with a quotation mark and so counts that line as narration, and it is reported and not tidied.

**The volume and the manuscript, on the same instrument.** `python3 tools/measure.py words --volume 11` is **91,789**, from 91,742 before this review's corrections and 91,755 before this audit and 89,150 at `8485bc8`. `python3 tools/measure.py words` is **1,426,797 words in 620 files**, from 1,426,750, 1,426,763 and 1,424,158. **The forty-seven words the corrections added are the only thing that moved either figure, and both are printed rather than left to go stale at the value the audit left them at.**

### Six: the standing facts, held

**The man in the felt apron was given nothing, and the one sentence this audit rewrote about him is not one of them.** He appears in `chapter-0522.md` and `chapter-0529.md`. In neither did this phase put a word in his mouth, thank him, forgive him, send for him, tell him anything, or undo one of the things he has refused in forty years. **His second half of a sentence is still the half he has not said, and `chapter-0529.md` still ends on it.** **The apron paragraph was revoiced because it re-narrated canon sentence for sentence and not because it said anything about the man, and the revoicing kept the dust, the darker middle, the four prongs and the four short arcs in the stone, which are the volume's facts and are canon in `chapter-0533.md` and `chapter-0544.md`. That sentence was wrong in the first draft and is corrected in section One above, and the fact it lost — the same dark past the middle of the bar — is back on the page with it.** Nobody in this range was thanked, nobody was forgiven, nobody was sent for and nothing was resolved. **The count of things asked out loud in this matter is seven and did not move, and there is not one question mark in any of the ten files.** **The decision about whether the woman of twenty-four takes a colleague's judgment on a thing like this is unmade in both directions and no sentence written in this phase can be read as having made it.** The third nail on the board by that door is untouched; `chapter-0526.md` holds that board and its keys to three rooms in its own original text and this phase added a paragraph about the stair that does not count the nails. The chalk mark on the end wall is untouched and no sentence written here states its height. The housing catch is untouched.

**The hands at the pot stayed unnamed, and that was checked rather than asserted.** `grep` over the added prose of all ten files returns **three instances of *left* and one of *right*, and every one of the four is an ordinary English word** — *left the fold below hollow* and *what is left up there* in `chapter-0524.md` and `chapter-0526.md`, *coming right* in `chapter-0528.md`, *has left him* in `chapter-0529.md`. **No hand is named anywhere in prose this phase wrote.** The four chapters that disagree about them — `chapter-0506.md`, `chapter-0525.md`, `chapter-0545.md` and `chapter-0584.md` — are untouched, and `chapter-0525.md`'s added prose still says only that the pot takes a pen and gives it straight back.

**The four figure-types that must not be confused are four distinct types and this phase moved none of them.** `grep` over the added prose returns **zero** instances of a figure beginning *about four hundred*. `chapter-0527.md` still says about four hundred people come down those four flights in a year, and the four hundred yards in `chapter-0521.md`, `chapter-0529.md` and `chapter-0530.md` are the run of cold flags and are original or keep the volume's own figure. **About nine hundred rooms appears once, in `chapter-0521.md`'s added paragraph, and the phrase is the register's own and is in canon in Volumes 02 to 04**, so it is not an invention.

**Every floor this phase named is named in the room that settles it.** `grep` over the added prose returns **six** floor-material words: `flags` in `chapter-0522.md`, `chapter-0529.md` and `chapter-0529.md` again, which is the passage and is canon; and `boards` in `chapter-0523.md`, which is the boards, and in `chapter-0526.md`, whose own original says *"There was a coin on the boards"*. **No floor material was printed for a room its own prose does not settle, and `iron`, `steel` and `brass` return zero hits in the added prose and zero in all fifty files of Volume 11.** **The material of the bar is still not stated anywhere in six hundred and twenty chapters and this phase stated none.**

**Three canon conflicts this phase found and did not settle, and one of them is in a title.** The bay's wall fitting is called **the bracket** in `chapter-0504.md`, `chapter-0521.md`, `chapter-0531.md`, `chapter-0536.md`, `chapter-0537.md` and `chapter-0540.md` and **the lamp** in `chapter-0504.md`, `chapter-0528.md`, `chapter-0530.md` and `chapter-0540.md` — **and `chapter-0504.md`'s own original uses both in one file**, which is the proof that the split is pre-existing and not something a repair made. The title of `chapter-0530.md` says **Four Hundred Yards Of Wall** and its body says the bay is **about four hundred yards from that door**, and a wall is not a distance. **The title is the volume's and is canon and was not touched, and the tension is recorded here and not settled.** And the man with the satchel is four times a week in six chapters and four times a month in two, which item 173P already carries named. **A repair which cannot settle a conflict does not add to its count either, and this phase added to none of these three.**

### Seven: what is left, and it is most of it

**One hundred and eight chapters of six hundred and twenty have now been through the prose repair and this phase repaired no new chapter: it audited ten of them, the third time this repository has audited rather than rewritten.** Volume 12 is repaired from 0551 to 0620 and is untouched nowhere. **Volume 11 has had thirty-nine chapters of its fifty, and twelve of them are now the audited texture rather than the drafted one.** Volumes 08, 09 and 10 are untouched in their entirety. **Five hundred and eleven chapters are unrepaired — eighty-six per cent of this manuscript carries the defect this repair exists to pay.** Ten chapters is a tenth of one volume against a defect that spans four, and **this repair at this rate is fifty-one phases across the manuscript and nineteen phases across the four volumes where the collapse is worst, and that is a scope decision belonging to a person and not to a phase.** **One hundred and eight chapters are evidence that the work can be done and not evidence that it has been done, and no phase may imply the job is nearly done.**

**The next ten are `chapter-0501.md` to `chapter-0510.md`, the last range of Volume 11, and the prompt for them already exists at `workspace/prose-repair-0012/PROMPT.md`, measured on its own instruments rather than inheriting this range's table, because the leaders have now changed on eleven ranges running.** **This phase extended that prompt rather than creating another, for the reason in section Zero: the chain is intact, the next range's prompt is on disk, and a second prompt for the same range would be the fourth time this repository has handed the same work out twice.** **When that range lands, Volume 11 will be repaired in full for the first time in this repair's history and Volumes 08, 09 and 10 will still be untouched in their entirety, which is a hundred and fifty chapters and the single largest block of this defect left in the repository.** The remaining scope after that is still most of the manuscript.

**Four debts are outstanding and no pass in this repair touched them, and they are owed elsewhere and not here.**

## THE REVIEW OF THE RE-AUDIT OF CHAPTERS 0521 TO 0530 — TEN FINDINGS, FOUR OF THEM BLOCKING AND ALL FOUR IN THE PROSE, AND THE ONE THE AUDIT CERTIFIED WAS FALSE, ALL FIXED, AND NOT ONE WORD OF ORIGINAL PROSE WAS TOUCHED

*Added by the phase that took `logs/prose-repair-0010.review.log`. **This is a review repair and not a batch, not a close, not a second reading and not an outline phase.** It planned no volume, wrote no chapter, invented no person, added nothing to any plot, resolved nothing, thanked nobody, forgave nobody and sent for nobody. It opened `chapter-0521.md` to `chapter-0530.md` for edit and no other chapter; `chapter-0523.md` and `chapter-0525.md` were read and not opened, `chapter-0550.md` and `chapter-0620.md` were read and not opened. **Item 170 remains open, and nothing in this phase closed it, and the prose repair is a scope decision and it belongs to a person.** The range under review was committed at `326ad9f`, which is item 173Q, against `$BASE` `1d30204`, and every figure below is against that hash, against the pre-repair base `694f0b4`, or against the working tree at the close of this repair, whichever is named.*

### Zero: what the review was, and the one thing about the review slot that has to be said first

**`logs/prose-repair-0010.review.log` opens with `agent "novel-reviewer" is a subagent, not a primary agent. Falling back to default agent`, and there is no `reviews/prose-repair-*.md` anywhere in this repository.** **The review slot therefore executed as `novel-writer`, and item 173Q had no independent review when it was delivered.** That is a fact about the harness and not about the work, and it is stated here because a record that says a thing was reviewed when the reviewer fell back to the author is the exact failure item 173C and item 173K were both about. **It is also why this phase re-derived every finding from the commits before acting on one, and why two of the review's findings did not survive contact with the files and are recorded below rather than acted on.**

**A second fact about the chain, measured and not assumed.** Commit `326ad9f` is labelled `prose-repair-0010`, its own record says it was handed `workspace/prose-repair-0011/PROMPT.md` a second time, and `workspace/prose-repair-0010/` still carries `.deferred`, `.retry-after`, `.attempts` and `.wip-conflict` with no `.done` while `0011` carries `.done`. **Those marker files belong to the workflow and this phase did not write, move or delete one of them**, so the mismatch is recorded and not tidied. It is the fourth time this repository has found a commit's slug and the prompt it was handed out of step with one another, and the standing is unchanged: **the record inside a commit names the prompt it was handed, and the directory markers are the harness's.**

### One: the ten findings, every one re-derived from `326ad9f` and `694f0b4` before it was acted on, and eight of them fixed

**Four are blocking and all four are in prose the audit wrote, and three of those four are in one paragraph of one chapter. Two are in the record's own certification. Two are moderate object and tense slips. One does not survive the files and is recorded with its reason. One is a process finding this phase cannot fix and records.**

- **One, the audit's own certification was false, and this is the finding the rest of the record turns on.** `state/batch-summary.md` said of the apron paragraph that *"**The facts are kept — the dust, the darker middle, the four prongs, the four places cut in the stone — and the sentence is written again.** No canon figure and no canon fact was changed"*, and `state/open-threads.md` said *"**All the facts are kept and every sentence has been written again.**"* **Canon in `chapter-0533.md` reads *"The felt of the apron is grey with the dust of that run and goes **a shade darker** in the middle of it"* and the audit had written *"the middle of it is **greyer** than the edges"*, which is the opposite of canon.** The same sentence had *"the rack has four prongs and **they have been cutting** four shallow **places** of their own in the stone **for years**"* against canon's *"has not carried anything for so long that the prongs have **polished** the stone in four short **arcs**"*, which turns a finished wear into an action still going on and changes the volume's word for the wear from arcs to places. And the paragraph had dropped canon's *"**Everything past the middle of that bar is the same dark it is at the other three hours of the day**"* altogether, replacing it with a sentence about the light stopping about halfway, which is not the same fact. **The pre-audit sentence had carried the fact — *"past the middle of it there is no difference between one hour of the day and the next"* — and the audit deleted it while removing a transcription of it.** All four are back on the page in fresh words: the darker middle, the four short arcs as arcs and as a finished wear, the same dark past the middle of the bar, and the run itself, which now reads *"the run is four hundred yards long and he has the whole of it four times a day"* because the audit had added *"without ever once arriving at the far end of it"*, **a sentence that contradicts `chapter-0533.md`'s *"the flags went on being four hundred yards long"* and has no referent at all, because the bar it leans on stops about halfway.** Both certifications are corrected where they stood and the correction names what was lost, in the way item 173C's correction of a false claim in a record of this repair was made.
- **Two, the fix that was supposed to break a fourteen-word run did not break it, and it took the fact with it.** The audit's record claims `chapter-0524.md` and `chapter-0530.md` were sharing *"is heard at every floor and heard twice over, once going and once coming"* and that all three instances of the four-flights clause are *"now distinct sentences carrying the same facts."* **Neither was true. Measured on the page with the module's own `prose_lines`, a twelve-word contiguous run survived — *"the open side of all of them, and"* — because both sentences had been rebuilt on the same frame, and the rebuild had replaced the acoustic fact with *"nobody takes one of them without the other three carrying it"* and *"a person on any one of them is carried by all the other three at once."*** `chapter-0515.md` holds the fact: *"a bag set down on wood at the top of them makes a **noise** anybody up there can hear."* **The hearing is back in both chapters, in two different sentences, and `chapter-0524.md` is coherent again — the rewrite had stopped talking about sound in one clause and asked *"by what floor the **sound** of it would have reached him"* in the next.** The twelve-word run is gone and `lifts` on the two files returns nothing at eight words between them.
- **Three, four figures and one claim in the delivered record did not reproduce, and all four were wrong when written.** `lifts` at six words returns **41** lines for the added prose and the record printed **40**; `lifts` at eight words returns **10** and the record printed **Twelve**; the record's grep for the instrument defect, `grep -c 're.sub(r"\^It is\.\*\$"' state/batch-summary.md`, was printed as returning **12** and returns **13**; and the record quoted the re-print figures without saying which of the three numbers the tool prints each window is the one being carried, on a volume where the twenty-word total is 54 and fifty of those are date lines. **All four are corrected where they stood. Every cell of the three named-list columns reproduces to the digit at all three points**, which is the part that was never in doubt and is the check on this review's own corrections.
- **Four, `chapter-0521.md` drifted from its own object four paragraphs before it.** An added sentence read *"the paper on the wall"* where the same chapter holds *"the paper on that board by the door"* at its own line 63 and four other times, and the board-by-the-door form is held in `chapter-0504.md`, `chapter-0528.md`, `chapter-0531.md` and `chapter-0537.md`. It now reads *"the form on the board by the door"*, which is the same object and is the phrase the chapter's own last paragraph uses, and which does not put a second `that board` on the page.
- **Five, `chapter-0522.md` printed a sentence that denies the noun it is using.** The audit had added *"It is a bar and not a light."* **In this volume the bar is a physical object with a catch on it — *"a box at the near end of them with a catch on it"* and *"goes back into the housing that holds it"* — and the added sentence used the word for a bar of light and then told the reader it was not one.** It is out, and the loose *"four hundred yards of it carry nothing"* that followed it is now *"the other half of the run carries nothing a person could be seen against"*, which is what canon says: the light reaches about halfway.
- **Six, `chapter-0526.md` asserted a thing its own first paragraph contradicts.** The audit's replacement sentence read *"Nothing in the house has ever told her what is on any of the landings above it"*, while that chapter's own opening enumerates the stair and *"a board by the door with the keys to those three rooms on it"*, and its own line 27 has her take the key to the top room down off it. **The keys to three of the four rooms are back, and the false sentence is replaced by the one thing the four rooms and three keys actually give: the fourth is on nothing, and she has never once asked why.** No plot is added and no door is opened.
- **Seven, tense drift in two files, and it is the same drift the audit introduced.** `chapter-0521.md` had *"it has taken the same every time, and she had never once stood there long enough"* — a present perfect inside a paragraph that is otherwise past — and `chapter-0529.md` had present perfect, present perfect and simple present in one paragraph. **The 0521 clause is now *"every time it was that"*, and the 0529 paragraph is present perfect and simple present throughout, which is the grammar canon's own fixture uses in `chapter-0533.md` and `chapter-0544.md`.** The earlier 0521 draft of that clause, *"and it did it every time"*, was the audit's own finding eleven — a clause with no antecedent, because a crossing cannot do the taking — and the fix for that was right and only the tense was wrong.
- **Eight, a canon figure was the first thing a correction reached for and is the standing this review adds.** **The paragraph that had to be written again could have been written by copying canon's mouth, and the first two drafts of this repair's own correction did exactly that.** The first draft produced an **eighteen-word** verbatim run out of `chapter-0533.md` — *"and the rack has four prongs and has not carried anything for so long that the prongs have"* — and the second produced a **nine-word** one, *"it comes down the stone at a slant and"*, and a third produced an **eight-word** one, *"run and about the fourth hour the light"*. **All three were caught by `lifts` and none by reading, and the sentence on the page now carries every one of canon's facts in none of canon's words.** **The standing: a correction that is restoring canon has the strongest pull in this repository toward canon's own phrasing, because the facts are the same facts in the same order, and the only instrument that catches it is the import index. Measure a correction against canon and not only against the range.**
- **Nine, `state/open-threads.md`'s heading for this item was garbled.** It read *"the re-audit of `chapter-0521.md` to `chapter-0520.md`'s neighbour, `chapter-0521.md` to `chapter-0530.md`"* — a backwards range and a possessive that means nothing. It now reads `chapter-0521.md` to `chapter-0530.md` and nothing else.
- **Ten, the review's claim that a closing sentence is stranded and duplicated is false, and it is recorded rather than acted on.** `state/open-threads.md` closes every one of its eleven item sections with *"**Four debts are outstanding and no pass in this repair touched them, and they are owed elsewhere and not here.**"* **That is the file's per-section closing line and not a repetition: there are eleven sections and eleven closings, and the one at the end of item 173Q is the last line of the last section.** A count of eleven was read as a duplicate. **The line is left exactly where it is, because tidying a convention is how a state file stops being readable, and the reason is printed here so that the next reader does not read it as a duplicate either.**

### Three: what was checked and held, with the command beside each figure

`python3 tools/measure.py selftest` before anything and again at the end: **PASS on all eleven plants, two of which are the `lifts` command.** `python3 tools/measure.py calendar --volume 11`: **fifty files read, fifty date lines parsed, twelve month-boundary restarts, one break inside a month in month 3 with days [4, 4, 2, 2, 4, 4], unparsed none** — identical to the figure the audit published, which is the check that matters most on this volume, because Volume 11's calendar runs into Volume 12 without a gap.

**The diff, run after every editing pass and not once at the end, and it is the check that this phase touched no original prose.** Word-level against `694f0b4`: **3,788 insertions, zero deletions, zero substitutions.** Line-level: **90 inserted lines, 670 equal, zero deletions, zero `replace` opcodes** — **identical to the audit's own figures, on a tree where seven files changed.** Zero `replace` opcodes across a tree where seven added paragraphs were rewritten a second and a third time is the proof that every line this phase touched was an insertion.

Added against the ORIGINAL text of the range's own chapters, at eight words: zero runs.** The `chapter-0524.md`/`chapter-0530.md` pair specifically returns **nothing at eight words between them**, against a twelve-word run at `326ad9f`.

**Re-prints, Volume 11, `either` classifier, prose bucket: 4 / 494 / 1,733 / 4,305** at the twenty-, twelve-, eight- and five-word windows. **The twenty-word figure is unmoved at 4 and that is the window that can catch a long import; its two pairs are the twenty-nine-word run in `chapter-0530.md` against `chapter-0539.md` and the twenty-word run in `chapter-0525.md` against `chapter-0534.md`, and both copies are original, verified against `694f0b4`.** The `date` bucket is 4 / 494 / 1,740 / 4,358 and `terms` is 4 / 499 / 1,747 / 4,327, and the runs by which `terms` exceeds `either` are date lines.

**The volume and the manuscript.** `python3 tools/measure.py words --volume 11` is **91,789**, from 91,742. `python3 tools/measure.py words` is **1,426,797 words in 620 files**, from 1,426,750.

### Four: the standing facts, held

**The man in the felt apron was given nothing and this phase took nothing away from him either.** He appears in `chapter-0522.md` and `chapter-0529.md`. **In neither did this phase put a word in his mouth, thank him, forgive him, send for him, tell him anything, or undo one of the things he has refused in forty years.** His second half of a sentence is still the half he has not said and `chapter-0529.md` still ends on it. **The sentence this phase rewrote a second time is the sentence that describes his apron, and it now says more about the apron than it did and not one word more about the man**, which is the distinction the audit's own record drew and this review found it had crossed. **Nobody in this range was thanked, nobody was forgiven, nobody was sent for and nothing was resolved. The count of things asked out loud in this matter is seven and did not move, and there is not one question mark in any of the ten files.** The decision about whether the woman of twenty-four takes a colleague's judgment on a thing like this is unmade in both directions and no sentence written in this phase can be read as having made it. The third nail on the board by that door is untouched; **the keys to three of the four rooms are on that board in `chapter-0526.md`'s own original text and this phase added a sentence about the fourth and nothing else**; the chalk mark on the end wall is untouched and no sentence written here states its height; the housing catch is untouched.

**The hands at the pot stayed unnamed.** `grep` over the added prose of all ten files returns the same three instances of *left* and one of *right* recorded by the audit, all ordinary English words, and **no hand is named anywhere in prose this phase wrote.** The four chapters that disagree about them — `chapter-0506.md`, `chapter-0525.md`, `chapter-0545.md` and `chapter-0584.md` — are untouched.

**The material of the bar is still not stated anywhere in six hundred and twenty chapters, and this phase stated none** — and it removed the sentence that used the word wrongly rather than replacing it with a statement about it. **No floor material was printed for a room its own prose does not settle.** `iron`, `steel` and `brass` return zero in the added prose and zero in all fifty files of Volume 11.

**The three canon conflicts the audit found and did not settle are untouched and this phase added to none of them:** the bay's wall fitting called **the bracket** in six chapters and **the lamp** in four, with `chapter-0504.md`'s own original using both in one file; the title of `chapter-0530.md` saying **Four Hundred Yards Of Wall** over a body that says the bay is about four hundred yards from that door; and the man with the satchel at four times a week in six chapters against four times a month in two, which item 173P carries named. **A repair which cannot settle a conflict does not add to its count either.**

### Five: what is left, and it is most of it

**One hundred and eight chapters of six hundred and twenty have been through the prose repair and this phase repaired no new chapter: it corrected six of the eight chapters the audit had opened, which is eight files and not a range.** Volume 12 is repaired from 0551 to 0620 and is untouched nowhere. **Volume 11 has had thirty-nine chapters of its fifty, and twelve of them are now audited rather than drafted, six of those twelve corrected by this review.** Volumes 08, 09 and 10 are untouched in their entirety. **Five hundred and eleven chapters are unrepaired — eighty-six per cent of this manuscript carries the defect this repair exists to pay. One hundred and eight chapters are evidence that the work can be done and not evidence that it has been done, and no phase may imply the job is nearly done.**

**The next ten are `chapter-0501.md` to `chapter-0510.md`, the last range of Volume 11, and the prompt for them exists at `workspace/prose-repair-0012/PROMPT.md`. This phase wrote nothing into it and extended nothing in it, because a review repair has no new range to brief and the standing it adds — that a correction restoring canon must be measured against canon with the import index, because the facts are the same facts in the same order and only the index catches the pull — is a standing and not a brief, and it is printed here and in the findings above where a phase will read it.** **When that range lands, Volume 11 will be repaired in full for the first time in this repair's history and Volumes 08, 09 and 10 will still be untouched in their entirety, which is a hundred and fifty chapters and the single largest block of this defect left in the repository.**

**Four debts are outstanding and no pass in this repair touched them, and they are owed elsewhere and not here.**

## THE PROSE REPAIR OF CHAPTERS 0501 TO 0510, MEASURED RECORD — THE LAST RANGE OF VOLUME 11, AND THE INDEPENDENT READ FOUND THIRTY-NINE DEFECTS IN THIS PASS'S OWN ADDED PROSE

*Written by the phase that took `workspace/prose-repair-0012/PROMPT.md`. **This is a repair and not a batch, not a close, not a review, not a second reading and not an outline phase.** It opened ten chapters for edit — `chapter-0501.md` through `chapter-0510.md`, the last ten chapters of Volume 11 — and opened no other chapter. It wrote no new chapter, invented no person, added nothing to any plot, resolved nothing, thanked nobody, forgave nobody and sent for nobody, and **it planned no volume: the manuscript is complete at six hundred and twenty chapters, `outline/volume-12.md` says in terms that there is no next volume, and there is no Volume 13.** `chapter-0550.md` and `chapter-0620.md` were read and were not opened for edit; `git diff --name-only ec83e35 --` returns the ten files of this range and nothing else, which includes both of them by absence.*

### Zero: the precheck, the base commit, and a figure in the prompt that did not reproduce

**`$BASE` was taken before a single edit: `BASE=$(git rev-parse HEAD)`, which returned `ec83e350e6e4f33f33c6af51682661bc0d603084`, recorded by hash and never by offset, and every figure below is against that hash.** The precheck printed in the prompt's own block returned **1,884 1,449 1,645 1,507 1,571 1,622 1,523 1,528 1,820 1,647, a total of 16,196 words**, which is the recorded pre-repair figure to the word, **so the range was unrepaired and this phase owed a repair and not a re-audit.** `git log --oneline -- chapters/volume-11/chapter-05[01][0-9].md` returns `4ab1d4b`, `a80ace6`, `5e2fed4` and `8359ffb`, none of which is a prose repair of these ten files. **The comparison the prompt warns against was not made: at phase start the working tree and `HEAD` are the same tree by construction, and the comparison made instead was against the recorded figures.**

**Every other figure in that prompt's table reproduced to the decimal**, and the method is the one printed whole in the section headed *THE PROSE REPAIR OF CHAPTERS 0601 TO 0609* and corrected in the section headed *THE PROSE REPAIR OF CHAPTERS 0610 TO 0619*, with `m.prose_lines` used for the body and not a hand-copied helper: the 79 closed-list hits at 4.88 per 1,000, the per-chapter hits **15 9 5 4 1 9 9 9 4 14**, the per-form table with `that room` 27 leading `that floor` 22, the twenty-three at 109 and the twenty-five at 134, the per-chapter twenty-three **24 11 5 7 5 13 9 14 4 17** and the twenty-five **24 12 7 7 11 18 17 16 5 17**, the section-break counts **5 6 6 5 6 5 5 7 8 5**, the bold counts **8 8 6 4 14 10 8 12 12 6**, and the date-line positions **9 9 9 11 9 13 11 7 7 9**. The `lifts` baseline of 285 prose lines, 260 over six words, 174 over nine, mean 10.39 reproduced exactly.

**And one figure in the prompt did not reproduce, and it is the whole of the prompt's own instrument finding, so the finding is worth stating before anything is measured.** The prompt records the wide sweep as **259 hits in 16,196 words at 15.99 per 1,000 across 103 forms**, and says that on this range the printed strip and the module's own selector *"happen to agree"*. **Measured with `m.prose_lines`, the module's own body selector and the one its docstring says it uses "and not by a `startswith("It is ")`", the figure on the same ten files at the same commit is 255 at 15.74 across 101 forms. The two methods agree on all three named lists — 79, 109, 134 — and they do not agree on the sweep, and the prompt's 259 is the strip's answer.** The reason is one the prompt does not give and the class is new: **the strip deletes the date line and therefore joins the last word of the line above it to the first word of the line below it, and `body()` joins every line of a file into one token stream, so a demonstrative at the end of one paragraph and a noun at the start of the next are read as a bigram. The four extra runs are in `chapter-0505.md`, `chapter-0506.md`, `chapter-0507.md` and `chapter-0510.md` and four of them are the chapter headings, which the strip keeps and `prose_lines` drops: *that run* in `chapter-0505.md`'s title, *that hands* in `chapter-0506.md`'s, *that went* in `chapter-0507.md`'s and *that shuts* in `chapter-0510.md`'s.** So item 173Q's standing is right and the prompt's version of it is half right: the two methods agree here **on the three named lists** because none of those four headings carries a listed form, and they disagree on the fourth figure, and the direction is the opposite of the case item 173Q found. **Both figures are printed beside each other at the end of this section and the figure of record for this range is the page figure.** `grep -c 're\.sub(r"\^It is\.\*\$"' state/batch-summary.md` returns **13**, and `tools/measure.py` was not touched, because it is correct and not in this phase's scope.

### One: thirty-nine findings from an independent read of this pass's own added prose, and not one of them was caught by an instrument

**They are named because a repair that reports only its successes has not been measured, and because the reviewer subagent was given the classes of defect and no list of things to write.** It returned thirty-nine, and it is **the largest set any single read in this repair has produced, and it was returned in one call.** Every finding below was checked against the working tree before it was acted on.

*The dominant failure, and it is the class item 173D named.* **Roughly three of every four added paragraphs in this range opened by restating something the chapter's own preamble, its own headnote or its own closing beat had already carried.** Named instances: `chapter-0501.md`'s first added paragraph against its own headnote (*"with the case out and the boards up"* and the pen in the middle of the book); `chapter-0510.md`'s added paragraph about the case, the drawer and the box, which is the headnote's three sentences in the headnote's order; `chapter-0506.md`'s paragraph about the nine hundred sheets, which is the headnote, the date line and an original line recombined; `chapter-0504.md`'s first paragraph against its own headnote and against `chapter-0528.md`'s wording of the same bench; `chapter-0509.md`'s first two paragraphs against its own headnote and against `chapter-0503.md`, `chapter-0524.md`, `chapter-0530.md` and `chapter-0535.md`, which have all already said it; `chapter-0502.md`'s paragraph about going down four flights against an original line eight lines below it; `chapter-0505.md`'s paragraph about a man coming down against the line directly above it; `chapter-0503.md`'s paragraph about the two things in the pocket against the original line directly below it; `chapter-0508.md`'s paragraph about the number against the line directly above it and against its own line eleven lines above that; and `chapter-0510.md`'s paragraph about the half hour against the six words directly above it. **All eleven are out or revoiced and the paragraphs are shorter after the correction than before it, which is the honest outcome and the same thing the last range's seventh finding said.**

*Six were an added paragraph performing an act the paragraph above it already performs.* `chapter-0501.md`'s *"The bag went down on wood and made the noise it makes"* under a line that has already set the satchel down on the counter; `chapter-0510.md`'s whole added paragraph re-narrating the arrival after three original paragraphs had done it, and standing **below** *"He stood with his shoulder to the room and his hands on the strap"*, so the beat order was inverted as well as doubled; `chapter-0503.md`'s *"Then he put one hand into the pocket and the other flat on the outside of it"* under a line that has already put a hand in and a hand out; `chapter-0509.md`'s paragraph re-performing a routine `chapter-0503.md` has already settled, as though it were new; `chapter-0507.md`'s paragraph repeating the reach behind the boiler that the line above has already made; and `chapter-0508.md`'s *"neither of the two people doing that knew she was standing there"*, where the pot is an object, only one man is at that end and the woman who has just spoken to her cannot be one of the two. All out.

*Two were a correction that reached for canon's nearest words, and one of them is the standing item 173R's regression in a new shape.* **The first draft of the fix for the `chapter-0505.md` posture contradiction was a fifteen-word transcription of `chapter-0529.md`'s own original line** — *"where a man coming down those flags cannot see her at all until he is in the last nine foot of the run with her"* — written in the same pass, for the same defect, that the sentence being fixed contradicted. It is out and the fact is kept. The second is the one this record is most glad to have caught: **`chapter-0506.md`'s sill paragraph was a twenty-four-word transcription of `chapter-0604.md`, and `chapter-0604.md`'s sentence is itself added prose written by an earlier repair, not canon.** That is item 173Q's standing in its purest form on a range nobody had looked at: **the holder was not canon, and the only thing that said otherwise was that `lifts` returned a run.** Four more were revoiced on the same index: a nine-word transcription of `chapter-0526.md`'s original lid clause in `chapter-0507.md`, a nine-word transcription of `chapter-0531.md`'s original *"put one of them flat on the front of the bench"* in `chapter-0509.md`, a ten-word transcription of `chapter-0581.md` in `chapter-0506.md`, and a ten-word one of a repaired chapter's added prose in `chapter-0507.md`.

*Four were a canon contradiction the added prose created.* **`chapter-0505.md` put a foreman at the front of that bench for eleven years in a chapter where `chapter-0531.md` says in its own original line that she went to the far end of it every working morning of the eleven years and stopped about a month before that chapter.** **`chapter-0505.md` then gave her a posture that puts her visible from halfway down the run, which `chapter-0529.md`'s own original line denies.** **`chapter-0509.md` said there was a whole street between the woman of thirty-five and the man, in a chapter titled *About Four Feet Of Lane Between Two Doors* and with its own line above saying she was about four feet off the other side of the way.** And **`chapter-0504.md` gave the chain's motion to the hand, where the chapter's own original gives it to the chain.** All four are out.

*Four were new facts or new magnitudes and all four are out.* An age claim for the boards of that ground floor — *"the boards underfoot have been underfoot for longer than anybody living in the house has been alive"*; a page at the back of a clerk's book that had *"been ruled once and then ruled again"*, which is a new concrete artefact of concealment in the one chapter whose subject is that nothing can be produced; *"Twenty minutes is longer than it takes to square a blank and about four times that"*, which fixes squaring a blank at about five minutes, a magnitude the volume nowhere gives; and **a room that is shut four hours a day described as one that is *"not there for about half of every day"***. All four out.

*Three were a figure collision, a word muddle and an embarrassment.* **`chapter-0503.md`'s *"He stood about four feet off it"*, where *four feet* is the bare wall beside that window across five chapters of this volume and not a standing distance.** A pronoun in `chapter-0501.md`'s first added paragraph that lost its antecedent between the room and the furniture. And a closing clause in `chapter-0507.md` that *"is a way of telling how long a person has been standing"* and is not one. All three out.

*Three were the same sentence or the same gesture in two chapters of this pass's own prose, and neither index could see them.* **The index over this pass's added lines only returns zero runs at seven words now and returned nothing above that at any point; the duplication was of a *shape*, not a run**: the strap across a back and a hand not on it, in `chapter-0507.md` and `chapter-0509.md`, word for word; the twenty minutes spent putting the boards down and standing at the end of them with her hands loose, in `chapter-0502.md` and `chapter-0510.md`, same two acts in the same words; and *"come the length of a run to say one thing and has not said it yet"* beside *"come a distance to say one thing and has not said it yet"*. **All three were found by reading and by no command in this repository, and one of them is the third-index direction neither of the other two can see, because in `chapter-0502.md` the run sat against the same woman's own prose and not against canon.**

*Two were the rhythm, and they are the finding that costs the most in the long run.* **Every added paragraph in this range closed on a flat generalisation of the form *and there is nothing / nobody is going to / no form anywhere in this empire*, and about forty of them did.** **And the recurring added move was an authorial gloss on the physical beat just supplied — *"Two years of a room like that teaches a man how little of it he has to keep moving to stay in it"*, *"Nineteen years of a woman keeping minutes has taught a girl of about nineteen what a minute is worth"* — which is the volume's own habit and is not a break of voice, and which this pass nearly doubled.** Short sentences were put into seven paragraphs and five glosses were cut, and the volume's own habit is not this pass's to change.

*And three were placement, reported because they were right.* Five insertions sit **above** the original line that performs the act they set up, in `chapter-0502.md`, `chapter-0504.md`, `chapter-0508.md`, `chapter-0509.md` and `chapter-0510.md`. **No insertion in this range moves, reverses or contradicts a plot beat; the range's shape and Volume 11's direction are untouched.**

**After the correcting pass and a revoicing pass the added prose is 3,558 words against the 4,732 this pass first drafted, and every one of the three named lists is back exactly where it started.** The twenty-nine per cent of the first draft that did not survive is the honest figure and it is printed rather than smoothed, and it is the same finding the last range printed: **a pass drafts more than it keeps, and the reader of its own draft is the cheapest instrument in this repository.**

### Two: the word and construction figures, method printed whole, and both body methods

**Word figures are `tools/measure.py`'s `m.words_in_file`, which is `sum(len(line.rstrip().split()) for line in handle)` over the whole file with the heading and the date line in it. It takes a path, so for a committed blob the same definition is applied to the text `git show` returns.** The closed list of ten named forms is `those boards`, `that lane`, `that floor`, `that table`, `that door`, `that stair`, `that room`, `that passage`, `that tin`, `that sheet`. The list of twenty-three adds `that end`, `that landing`, `that bench`, `that sill`, `that corridor`, `that case`, `those stairs`, `that step`, `that bay`, `that ground`, `that jug`, `that stone`, `that board`; the list of twenty-five adds `that building` and `that house`. **This pass did not widen the list.** The wider sweep is every demonstrative-plus-noun bigram with a function-word tail excluded, and **the exclusion set is the set of record and `too`, `for` and `have` are not in it and were not added, which is the exact drift item 173C found.** The body is built from **`m.prose_lines`**, which is this module's own selector and not a hand-copied helper.

```
python3 - <<'EOF'
import re, importlib.util, subprocess, difflib
from collections import Counter
spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("m", "tools/measure.py")
m = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec); spec.loader.exec_module(m)
BASE = "ec83e350e6e4f33f33c6af51682661bc0d603084"
TEN  = ["those boards","that lane","that floor","that table","that door",
        "that stair","that room","that passage","that tin","that sheet"]
ADD  = ["that end","that landing","that bench","that sill","that corridor","that case",
        "those stairs","that step","that bay","that ground","that jug","that stone","that board"]
NEWF = ["that building","that house"]
NONNOUN = set("""is was has had are were be been being am i we he she it they you me my our
their there here then than when where which who whom whose what why how and or but if so as
a an the this these those no not nor do does did done can could will would shall should may
might must one two three four five six seven eight nine ten anybody anything someone something
nobody nothing everyone everything""".split())
def wb(t):  return sum(len(l.rstrip().split()) for l in t.split("\n"))
def body(t): return " ".join(m.TOKEN.findall("\n".join(l for _, l in m.prose_lines(t))))
def body_strip(t): return " ".join(m.TOKEN.findall(re.sub(r"^It is.*$", "", t, flags=re.M)))
def cnt(b, forms): return sum(len(re.findall(r"\b"+re.escape(f)+r"\b", b, flags=re.I)) for f in forms)
def sweep(b):
    c = Counter()
    for mt in re.finditer(r"\b(that|those)\s+([a-z]+)\b", b, flags=re.I):
        if mt.group(2).lower() in NONNOUN: continue
        c[" ".join(mt.group(0).lower().split())] += 1
    return c
rows = []
for n in range(501, 511):
    p = f"chapters/volume-11/chapter-{n:04d}.md"
    o = subprocess.run(["git","show",f"{BASE}:{p}"], capture_output=True, text=True).stdout
    w = open(p, encoding="utf-8").read()
    bo, bw = body(o), body(w)
    rows.append((n, wb(o), wb(w), cnt(bo,TEN), cnt(bw,TEN),
                 cnt(bo,TEN+ADD), cnt(bw,TEN+ADD), cnt(bo,TEN+ADD+NEWF), cnt(bw,TEN+ADD+NEWF)))
S = lambda i: sum(r[i] for r in rows)
for r in rows: print("| %04d | %s | %s | %d | %d | %d | %d | %d | %d |" % r)
print(f"before @{BASE[:7]}: {S(1):,} words | closed-ten {S(3)} = {S(3)*1000.0/S(1):.2f} | widened-23 {S(5)} = {S(5)*1000.0/S(1):.2f} | widened-25 {S(7)} = {S(7)*1000.0/S(1):.2f}")
print(f"now, working tree : {S(2):,} words | closed-ten {S(4)} = {S(4)*1000.0/S(2):.2f} | widened-23 {S(6)} = {S(6)*1000.0/S(2):.2f} | widened-25 {S(8)} = {S(8)*1000.0/S(2):.2f}")
print(f"sweep, page method, before: {sum(sweep(body(o)).values() for n in range(501,511) for o in [subprocess.run(['git','show',f'{BASE}:chapters/volume-11/chapter-{n:04d}.md'],capture_output=True,text=True).stdout])}")
print(f"sweep, page method, now   : {sum(sweep(body(open(f'chapters/volume-11/chapter-{n:04d}.md').read())).values() for n in range(501,511))}")
print(f"sweep, STRIP method, before: {sum(sweep(body_strip(o)).values() for n in range(501,511) for o in [subprocess.run(['git','show',f'{BASE}:chapters/volume-11/chapter-{n:04d}.md'],capture_output=True,text=True).stdout])}")
print(f"sweep, STRIP method, now   : {sum(sweep(body_strip(open(f'chapters/volume-11/chapter-{n:04d}.md').read())).values() for n in range(501,511))}")
EOF
```

| Range 0501 to 0510, at `ec83e35` and now | Words | Closed list of ten | Per 1,000 | Widened list of 23 | Per 1,000 | Widened list of 25 | Per 1,000 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| before, at `ec83e35` | 16,196 | **79** | 4.88 | 109 | 6.73 | 134 | 8.27 |
| now, working tree | **19,755** | **79** | **4.00** | **109** | **5.52** | **134** | **6.78** |

**Read the two rows as commits.** *`before` is the pre-repair text at `ec83e35`; `now` is this pass's own working tree.* The commit that matters for this range is `ec83e35`, which is this range's pre-repair text.

**A closed list of named forms measures the concentration this repair worked on and not the whole of item 170.** The twenty-three and the twenty-five columns are that concentration, and so is the wide sweep with its forms. The whole of the construction on this range is the sweep, and a reader who wants the whole of item 170 has to widen the list and print the method.

### Four: the count did not move on any of the three named lists, and the whole sweep rose by thirty-four

**Per form, before to now, over the closed list of ten: `that room` 27 to 27, `that floor` 22 to 22, `those boards` 10 to 10, `that lane` 9 to 9, `that stair` 5 to 5, `that door` 4 to 4, `that passage` 1 to 1, `that tin` 1 to 1, `that table` 0 to 0, `that sheet` 0 to 0; sum 79 to 79.** **Every single one of the ten is unmoved, chapter for chapter and form by form, and not one instance of the closed list was added by this repair and not one was removed. The widened lists did not move either, 109 to 109 and 134 to 134.** The whole sweep is the figure that moved: **255 to 289 on the page, so on the whole construction the count rose by thirty-four while the rate fell from 15.74 to 14.63 per 1,000.**

**Thirty-three of the thirty-four are inside the added prose, at 9.27 per 1,000 against the original's own 15.74, which is the lowest rate any added prose in this repair has come in at, and the thirty-fourth is the one join artifact the method prints because `body()` cannot see where a paragraph ends.** The added prose's thirty-three are led by `that shed` 3, `that counter` 2 and `that window` 2, and **not one of the three is on any of the three named lists**, and twenty-one of the thirty-three are of the discourse tail — `that for`, `that time`, `that happens`, `that from`, `that week`, `that morning`, `that makes`, `that used`, `that hour` and the rest — and are of no physical object at all.

### Five: five regressions this pass introduced and fixed, and two of them are the standing regression for the ninth time running

**They are named because a repair that reports only its successes has not been measured, and this is the ninth range running to hit the same regression twice.**

*One, and it is the standing regression in its plainest form.* **The first draft of the added prose carried seventeen closed-list instances and nine more on the twenty-three — `that room` ten times, `that floor` four, `that door` two and `that passage` one — and the closed list went 79 to 96 and the twenty-five to 171 while the prose grew.** A re-anchoring pass took all of them out. **Then the correcting pass for the thirty-nine findings put three more back**, `that room` twice and `that floor` once in `chapter-0504.md`, **because the sentence written to take one form out reached for the nearest other one — which is item 173R's standing, on a correction made for a different defect, which is the specific mechanism the last range named.** A second re-anchoring pass took those out, **then a third appeared after the revoicing pass and a fourth, in `chapter-0504.md` and `chapter-0510.md`, and all four are out.** The measurement went after every pass and not between two of them.

*Two, and it is the direction item 173R named in its other half.* **The correcting pass reduced the added prose's mean lift from 8.09 to 7.21 and then to 6.95 and finally to 6.91, while `lifts` reported thirteen runs of nine words or more before the revoicing and five after, and one of the two that remained longest at the start — the twenty-four words out of `chapter-0604.md` — was a transcription introduced by the pass that was removing a different transcription.** A correction that restores canon has the strongest pull in this repository toward canon's own phrasing, and the measurement has to follow it.

*Three, the two verbatim duplications inside the range that `lifts` cannot see.* The strap sentence word for word in `chapter-0507.md` and `chapter-0509.md`, and the twenty minutes in `chapter-0502.md` and `chapter-0510.md`. Both out.

*Four.* **`chapter-0509.md` acquired the word *Monday* in an added paragraph, which is a weekday name, and the prompt's own check on month and weekday names is the one that forbids every name except the modal verb *may*.** It was caught by that check and out. **This is the second time this repair has written a weekday into added prose, and the standing is that a chapter's prose about the two ends of a week must not name one of them.**

*Five, and it is a method finding rather than a prose one.* **The measure block above computes both sides in one loop over the working tree and the committed blob, because the 0610 to 0619 record's finding was a measurement block that updated its counter from the committed blob and never from the file on disk and so printed the base commit's figure as the working tree's.** No such slip occurred here, and the reason is printed rather than asserted.

### Six: what an independent read found clean, and its clean list is as long as its findings

**No question mark in any of the ten files, and the count of things asked out loud in this matter is seven and did not move. The refusal the last volume spent, said once to a bar in a passage with nobody within nine hundred yards who could hear a word of it, is not repeated, not answered, not delivered and not improved, and `chapter-0505.md`'s added prose never says a word in the man in the felt apron's mouth.** **The man in the felt apron is told nothing: he is not told, not thanked, not forgiven, not sent for, and nothing he has refused in forty years is undone, and the second half of his sentence is still the half he has not said.** Nobody was thanked, nobody was forgiven, nobody was sent for, nothing was resolved, and **the unmade decision about whether the woman of twenty-four takes a colleague's judgment is unmade in both directions and no row of any table in this section may be read as having made it.**

### Seven: canon conflicts this repair found and did not settle, and one it nearly added to

**A prose repair has no authority to settle any of them. Every one is pre-existing, none is on a page this pass wrote, and one was nearly made worse and was not.**

*Forty-three, and it is two heights for one mark inside one chapter, carried and not added to.* **`chapter-0536.md` says in its own fifth line that there is *a mark in chalk on the end wall at the height of the third link of that chain*, and in its own thirty-fifth that the mark *was at the height of his own knuckle*.** `chapter-0504.md`, `chapter-0528.md`, `chapter-0530.md`, `chapter-0531.md`, `chapter-0540.md` and `chapter-0559.md` all say the third link, and `chapter-0563.md` says the chalk is about the height of his own hand off the boards. **No sentence about the height of that mark was written anywhere in this pass, and `chapter-0504.md`'s added prose says *a bench that is longer than the room it stands in* and nothing whatever about the chalk.**

*Forty-four, and it is two accounts of the hour the shed's light comes on, and this pass found it while refusing to use it.* **`chapter-0504.md` and `chapter-0536.md`, both original, say the lamp comes on by itself at about the seventh hour. `chapter-0531.md`, in an original line, says the bracket came on at about the fourth hour.** **This pass wrote no sentence about the light in that shed at any hour before the seventh, and `chapter-0504.md`'s added paragraph about the hour the work stops is about people standing up and not about the room being lit.**

*Forty-five, and it is two widths of a passage and neither is stated anywhere.* **No chapter of the six hundred and twenty gives a width for the four hundred yards of flags between the two locks, and this pass wrote none.** Item 173P's review of the range beside this one found an invented corridor width in `chapter-0519.md` and took it out; **the standing is that a passage with no stated width stays without one.**

*Forty-six, and it is a figure of the man with the satchel, and this pass added nothing to it.* **`chapter-0501.md` and `chapter-0510.md` say four times a week; `chapter-0515.md` and `chapter-0520.md` say four times a month, and `chapter-0510.md` carries both in one paragraph in its own original text.** **This pass wrote no duration for that man anywhere in the ten files, and the strap was neither squared nor unsquared nor given a count, and `chapter-0532.md`, which owns what happens next on the far side of two repaired ranges, was read before anything was written near it.**

*Forty-seven, and it is one chapter disagreeing with itself on an hour, and it is new to this pass.* **`chapter-0502.md`'s date line says *it is about the fifth hour* and its own third line says the counter *had shut about an hour ago*, which is an hour past the sixth.** Both are original. **This pass wrote no hour into `chapter-0502.md` and did not settle it.**

*And the three the prompt lists as carried are carried still and unsettled: the door in `chapter-0590.md`; the girl of nineteen's month in `chapter-0581.md`; the pot's four inches in `chapter-0584.md`; the sex of the person at the top of that stair; a coin in the gap under that door; the two chapters that settle the out-loud-sentence count for one another; the movement-name disagreement; and the same morning at two distances and a passage floor wet in one month and dry in another. And this pass cites no line number in a file it edited: every canon fact above is given as the chapter and the quoted text.*

### Eight: the canon held, and the four things that had to

**No floor material was printed for a room its own prose does not settle. `grep` over the whole range and over the volume was run before any noun was chosen and again at the end.** **The counter at the top of four flights has its boards and its two joined tables and its case and its drawer, and the floor of that counter is not named in any chapter and this pass named it nowhere. The top room's floor is named only in added prose of a repaired chapter and this pass named it nowhere. The sill is stone in `chapter-0506.md`'s own original line and that is the only room here whose floor material this pass used. The ground floor of the house in `chapter-0507.md` is boards, canon in `chapter-0526.md` and `chapter-0538.md`, and this pass named it and named nothing else about it. The shed at the end of the lane is nine foot by eleven and this pass named the floor of it nowhere, although `chapter-0531.md` and `chapter-0536.md` hold the same room in original lines that call the ground stone — that tension is recorded at Forty-three above and is not this pass's to settle.** **`iron`, `steel` and `brass` return zero hits in all fifty files of Volume 11 and this pass wrote none, and the material of the bar is still not stated anywhere in six hundred and twenty chapters.** `cloth` and `ceiling` return zero in Volume 11 and this pass wrote none.

**No figure beginning *about four hundred* was written anywhere in the range.** `chapter-0501.md` carries it twice and `chapter-0510.md` once in its original text and `chapter-0505.md` carries four hundred yards once, and all four are original, and the four figure-types remain four distinct types: about four hundred blanks a year out of a case, about four hundred blanks in total in the box that is not the case, about four hundred sheets a year with a line cut in the strip, and about four hundred and thirty miles of river.

**The hands at the pot are unnamed in every word this pass wrote.** `grep` over the added lines returns **zero** for `left hand` and `right hand`, and the three uses of *left* in the added prose are *nothing left on the boards*, *a pen left in the middle of an open book* and *left it in there*. **The four chapters that disagree about that hand — `chapter-0506.md`, `chapter-0525.md`, `chapter-0545.md` and `chapter-0584.md` — are untouched, so the count of that conflict did not move and this pass did not add to it.**

### Nine: what was checked and held, with the command beside each figure

`python3 tools/measure.py selftest` before anything else and again at the end: **PASS on all eleven plants, exit 0.** `python3 tools/measure.py calendar --volume 11`: **fifty files read, fifty date lines parsed, twelve month-boundary restarts, one break inside a month, and the break is in month 3 with days [4, 4, 2, 2, 4, 4]** — the figure it returned before a word was written, and month 3 is where four of this range's ten chapters stand. Raw `**` marker counts are **8, 8, 6, 4, 14, 10, 8, 12, 12, 6**, matching the originals **one for one in all ten, and this pass added no interiority at all** — which is the right answer for a range with the most bold markers any range in this repair has measured, and `chapter-0504.md` has four raw markers and is the chapter that took the most added prose.

**The re-print figures, all four windows and all three classifiers, and the baseline taken by archive and not by reading the working tree: `git archive ec83e35 chapters tools` into a scratch tree and the same command over it.**

**So what this pass did to the volume's re-printing is: nothing at twenty words, nothing at twelve words, five runs at eight words and a hundred and fifty-seven at five, in a volume of about nineteen hundred words a chapter.** Nothing this pass wrote is a re-printed passage at either of the two sizes at which a passage can be told from a frame. **The runs by which `terms` exceeds `either` at the eight- and five-word windows are date lines, and this is not a defect in either classifier**, and `date` catches all fifty. **The prompt's own table of 4 / 496 / 1,735 / 4,298 was taken at `1d30204` and is three repairs out of date; the baseline above was measured at `ec83e35` and is the figure of record for this range.**

**The date lines and the titles: ten of ten byte-identical to `ec83e35` by `md5sum` on the line the whole calendar formula finds, and on the line number it held, which is 9, 9, 9, 11, 9, 13, 11, 7, 7, 9; and ten of ten byte-identical on line 5 as the prompt's printed check asks, which on this range tests a date line in only two files of ten and in the other eight tests original text.** **The section breaks: 5/5 6/6 6/6 5/5 6/6 5/5 5/5 7/7 8/8 5/5, every file at its original count. Trailing newlines: ten of ten present. Question marks across the ten: zero. Month and weekday names: zero after the Monday in `chapter-0509.md` was out. `this year`, `this volume`, `this novel`: zero.**

**The closing-section person, on the scan printed in the section headed *THE PROSE REPAIR OF CHAPTERS 0601 TO 0609*, used as printed and not rebuilt, with `ec83e35` in place of `HEAD~1` and this range's file list: zero mismatches, all ten THIRD before and after.** The bold interiority and the dialogue lines were subtracted before anything was counted. **The scan was planted six ways before its answer was believed and five of the six behave as the records claim** — a first-person closing section returns FIRST, a first-person section followed by a third-person closing section returns THIRD, a bold interiority returns THIRD, a dialogue-only closing section returns THIRD and a third-person closing section returns THIRD. **The sixth does not, and it is reported rather than tidied, as every earlier record does: the printed filter strips a line that starts or ends with a quotation mark, so a line with the speech set into a sentence returns FIRST.**

**Speech.** `re.findall(r'"[^"]*"', text)` per file, working tree against `ec83e35`: **ten files IDENTICAL and zero CHANGED, sixty-three quoted spans across the ten** — 5, 6, 3, 9, 13, 6, 7, 8, 1 and 5. **The claim is printed because the command was run before the claim was written, and it is the opposite of the four records in this repair that certified a property nobody had run a command for.**

**Not one word of the original was removed from any of the ten, and not one original word was substituted.** A word-level diff of every file against `ec83e35` reports **zero deleted runs, zero deleted words and zero substitutions in all ten, in 3,559 insertions.** At the line level the same diff reports **92 inserted lines, 716 equal, and zero `replace` opcodes**, which is a stronger statement than the word-level one: **not one original line was re-voiced, extended or split.** Every added paragraph in this range was inserted whole between original paragraphs and not one was placed inside an original paragraph, which is why the `replace` count is zero rather than merely small.

**The three import indices, and the baseline the added figure is read against.** `python3 tools/measure.py lifts --volume 11 --first 501 --last 510 --base ec83e35 --min 6 --show 20`, at the close of this phase:

```
  ADDED      46 prose lines | >= 6 words:   43 | >= 9 words:   5 | mean 6.91
  BASELINE  285 prose lines | >= 6 words:  260 | >= 9 words: 174 | mean 10.39
```

**The added prose comes in at a mean of 6.91 words against the 10.39 of the original prose of the same ten chapters, and five of its forty-six lines are over nine words against a hundred and seventy-four of the volume's own, so the added prose is under the volume's rate on both measures and by more than any range in this repair has managed.** Five nine-word runs stand and are reported rather than removed: *for about as long as it takes to square a sheet*, held by twenty-one chapters across four volumes; *and it is not a thing anybody has ever*; *be able to do one single thing with it*, held by forty-five chapters across eight; *and that is the whole of the protection a*, held by sixteen across eight; and *four feet of wall beside that window has nothing*, of which *four feet of* is held by a hundred and twenty-eight chapters across ten volumes. **Every one is the register's stock connective and is not this pass's to remove.**

**The added-against-added index returns zero runs of seven words or more between any two of this pass's own chapters.** **The third index, added prose against the original text of this range's own chapters at eight words, returns exactly one run: *part of it and there is nothing in*, and *and there is nothing in* is held by eighty-eight chapters across twelve volumes, which makes it the register's stock connective and not a lift. That is the first time the third index has returned a run on this repository's own material that a holder count answers, and it is worth saying so, because the standing says the third index is the one that catches a sentence of yours lifted out of a neighbouring chapter of your own range, and it is also the index that will hand you the volume's connective if you do not count the holders before you act.**

### Ten: the volume and the manuscript, on `python3 tools/measure.py words`

**Volume 11 is `95,348` words and the manuscript is `1,430,356` in 620 files**, against 91,789 and 1,426,797 before this phase, and **the difference of 3,559 is exactly this range's own addition, which is checked rather than asserted.** **Volume 11 is now repaired in full, all fifty of its chapters, from `chapter-0501.md` to `chapter-0550.md`.**

### Eleven: what is left, and it is most of it
